Planar graphs are acyclically edge ( Δ + 5 ) (\Delta+5) -colorable
Qiaojun Shu, Guohui Lin

Abstract
An edge coloring of a graph G G is to color all the edges in the graph such that adjacent edges receive different colors. It is acyclic if each cycle in the graph receives at least three colors. Fiamčik (1978) and Alon, Sudakov and Zaks (2001) conjectured that every simple graph with maximum degree Δ \Delta is acyclically edge ( Δ + 2 ) (\Delta+2) -colorable — the well-known acyclic edge coloring conjecture (AECC). Despite many major breakthroughs and minor improvements, the conjecture remains open even for planar graphs. In this paper, we prove that planar graphs are acyclically edge ( Δ + 5 ) (\Delta+5) -colorable. Our proof has two main steps: Using discharging methods, we first show that every non-trivial planar graph must have one of the eight groups of well characterized local structures; and then acyclically edge color the graph using no more than Δ + 5 \Delta+5 colors by an induction on the number of edges. Keywords: Acyclic edge coloring; planar graph; local structure; discharging; induction



Acknowledgments.

This research is supported by the NSERC Canada and the China Scholarship Council.

1
Introduction

We consider simple and
2
-connected graphs in this paper,
i.e., graphs without self-loops, without parallel edges but with at least two vertex-disjoint paths between any pair of vertices,
as explained below.

We use
V(G)
and
E(G)
to denote the vertex set and the edge set of a graph
G
, respectively,
and simplify them as
V
and
E
respectively when the graph is clear from the context.
This way,
G
is also denoted as
G=(V,E)
.

For an integer
k\geq 2
, we use the integers
1,2,\ldots,k
to represent the
k
distinct colors.
An
edge
k
-coloring
of the graph
G
is a mapping
c:E(G)\to\{1,2,\ldots,k\}
such that any two adjacent edges receive different colors.
If such a mapping exists, then
G
is said
edge
k
-colorable
.
The
chromatic index
of
G
, denoted as
\chi^{\prime}(G)
, is the smallest integer
k
such that
G
is edge
k
-colorable.
An edge
k
-coloring of
G
is called
acyclic
if each cycle receives at least three different colors,
or equivalently there is no
bichromatic
cycle in
G
, or equivalently the subgraph of
G
induced by any two colors is a forest.
Correspondingly,
a^{\prime}(G)
denotes the smallest integer
k
such that
G
is acyclically edge
k
-colorable, called the
acyclic chromatic index
of
G
.

One sees from the definition of acyclic edge coloring the reason why we consider simple and connected graphs only.
Furthermore, a connected graph can be represented as a block-cut tree,
in which each block is a
2
-connected component of the graph and two blocks are adjacent if and only if they overlap at a cut-vertex.
Given an acyclic edge
k
-coloring of the graph
G
, after swapping any two colors inside a block it remains as an acyclic edge
k
-coloring.
Therefore, we may constrain ourselves on
2
-connected graphs.
In the sequel, the input graph is simple and
2
-connected.

We use
\Delta(G)
to denote the maximum degree of the graph
G
, and simply as
\Delta
when the graph is clear from the context.
It holds trivially that
\Delta\leq\chi^{\prime}(G)\leq a^{\prime}(G)
.
By Vizing’s theorem
[
17
]
,
\chi^{\prime}(G)\leq\Delta+1
;
it follows from
\chi^{\prime}(K_{3})=3=\Delta+1
that
\Delta+1
is tight for the chromatic index
\chi^{\prime}(G)
.
For the acyclic chromatic index, one sees that
a^{\prime}(K_{4})=5=\Delta+2
.
Fiamčik
[
7
]
and Alon, Sudakov and Zaks
[
2
]
independently conjectured that it is upper bounded by
\Delta+2
,
called the
acyclic edge coloring conjecture
(AECC):

Conjecture 1

(AECC)

For any graph
G
,
a^{\prime}(G)\leq\Delta+2
.

The AECC has been confirmed true for graphs with
\Delta\in\{3,4\}

[
16
,
3
,
4
,
15
,
19
]
.
However, for an arbitrary graph
G
, the conjecture is far away from reached.
Yet, several major milestones bounding
a^{\prime}(G)
have been achieved:
Alon, McDiarmid and Reed
[
1
]
proved that
a^{\prime}(G)\leq 64\Delta
by a probabilistic argument;
and then the upper bound was improved to
16\Delta

[
9
]
, to
\lceil 9.62(\Delta-1)\rceil

[
10
]
, to
4\Delta-4

[
6
]
,
and most recently in 2017 to
\lceil 3.74(\Delta-1)\rceil+1
by Giotis et al.
[
8
]
using Lovász local lemma.

A
planar
graph is one that can be drawn in the two-dimensional plane so that its edges intersect only at their ending vertices.
The AECC on planar graphs also attracts many studies with much tighter upper bounds.
For an arbitrary planar graph
G
, Basavaraju et al.
[
5
]
first showed that
a^{\prime}(G)\leq\Delta+12
;
and then the upper bound was improved to
\Delta+7
by Wang, Shu and Wang
[
21
]
and the latest to
\Delta+6
by Wang and Zhang
[
18
]
.
For some sub-classes of planar graphs, the AECC has been confirmed true,
for example, planar graphs without
i
-cycles for each
i\in\{3,4,5,6\}

[
14
,
20
,
13
,
22
]
,
and planar graphs without intersecting triangles (i.e.,
3
-cycles)
[
11
,
12
]
.

In this paper, we focus on arbitrary planar graphs, aiming to improve the upper bound from
\Delta+6
to
\Delta+5
, stated in the following theorem.

Theorem 1

If
G
is a planar graph, then
a^{\prime}(G)\leq\Delta+5
.

Recall that when
\Delta\leq 4
, the AECC has been confirmed true.
We thus prove Theorem
1
assuming
\Delta\geq 5
.
The rest of the paper is organized as follows.
In Section 2, we characterize eight groups of local structures (also called
configurations
),
and by discharging methods we prove that
G
must contain at least one of these local structures.
We prove the theorem in Section 3 by an induction on the number of edges in
G
,
where we employ an important known property of an acyclic edge coloring (Lemma
6
) from
[
15
]
.
We conclude the paper in Section 4.

2
Notations and the eight groups of local structures

We fix a simple and
2
-connected planar graph
G
with
\Delta\geq 5
for discussion.

Let
d(v)
denote the degree of the vertex
v
in
G
.
A vertex of degree
k
(at least
k
, at most
k
, respectively) is called a
k
-
vertex
(
k^{+}
-
vertex
,
k^{-}
-
vertex
, respectively).
For convenience, let
n_{k}(v)
(
n_{k^{+}}(v)
,
n_{k^{-}}(v)
, respectively) denote the number of
k
-vertices (
k^{+}
-vertices,
k^{-}
-vertices, respectively) adjacent to
v
in
G
.

We use
[a,b]
to denote the set of integers in between
a
and
b
, inclusive.
Let
S_{i}
be a set of integers,
i=1,2
;
then an
(S_{1},S_{2})
vertex
is a
k
-vertex
x
of
G
such that
k\in S_{1}
and
n_{6^{+}}(x)\in S_{2}
(and in this case, we say that
x
is
(S_{1},S_{2})
).
When
S_{1}=\{k\}
(
S_{2}=\{j\}
,
S_{1}=\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
,
S_{2}=\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
, respectively), the above notation is simplified as
(k,S_{2})
(
(S_{1},j)
,
(k_{1}/k_{2},S_{2})
,
(S_{1},j_{1}/j_{2})
, respectively).
Similarly, when
S_{1}=[k,\infty]
(
S_{1}=[1,k]
,
S_{2}=[j,\infty]
,
S_{2}=[1,j]
, respectively),
we can define a
(k^{+},S_{2})
(
(k^{-},S_{2})
,
(S_{1},j^{+})
,
(S_{1},j^{-})
, respectively) vertex.
For instance,
(9,5)
is a degree-
9
vertex
x
having exactly five
6^{+}
-neighbors,
and
(7^{+},4^{-})
is a
7^{+}
-vertex
x
with no more than four
6^{+}
-neighbors.

Figure 1:
Some local structures of the groups (
A_{2}
)–(
A_{8}
) as defined in Theorem
2
.
In each local structure, filled vertices are distinct from each other and each has its degree determined,
while a non-filled vertex has an undetermined degree (i.e., could be incident with more edges not shown).
In (
A_{6.1}
) and (
A_{6.3}
), a dashed edge indicates the existence of a possible edge incident at the vertex.

Theorem 2

The graph
G
contains at least one of the following local structures
(
A_{1}
)–(
A_{8}
)
, most of which are depicted in Figure
1
.

(
A_{1}
)

A path
uvw
with
d(v)=2
such that at least one of the following holds:

(
A_{1.1}
)

uw\not\in E(G)
;

(
A_{1.2}
)

uw\in E(G)
and
d(u)\leq 7
;

(
A_{1.3}
)

uw\in E(G)
and
n_{5^{-}}(u)\geq d(u)-6
.

(
A_{2}
)

An edge
uv
with
N(v)=\{u,v_{1},v_{2}\}
such that at least one of the following holds:

(
A_{2.1}
)

d(u)\leq 7
;

(
A_{2.2}
)

u
is
([8,11],5^{-})
, and
uv_{1},uv_{2}\in E(G)
,
v_{1}v_{2}\not\in E(G)
;

(
A_{2.3}
)

u
is
(8,4^{-})
,
v_{2}
is
([8,11],5^{-})
, and
uv_{1},v_{1}v_{2}\in E(G)
,
uv_{2}\not\in E(G)
.

(
A_{2.4}
)

d(u)=8
, and
uv_{1},uv_{2},v_{1}v_{2}\in E(G)
.

(
A_{3}
)

A
(9,5^{-})
vertex
u
adjacent to a
3
-vertex
v
with
N(v)=\{u,v_{1},v_{2}\}
and
uv_{1},uv_{2},v_{1}v_{2}\in E(G)
such that at least one of the following holds:

(
A_{3.1}
)

d(v_{i})-n_{2}(v_{i})\neq\Delta
for each
i\in\{1,2\}
;

(
A_{3.2}
)

v_{2}
is
([9,13],6^{-})
, and
d(v_{1})-n_{2}(v_{1})\neq\Delta
or
n_{6^{+}}(v_{1})\leq\Delta-d(v_{2})+6
;

(
A_{3.3}
)

u
is
(9,4^{-})
and
v_{2}
is
([9,13],5^{-})
;

(
A_{3.4}
)

u
is
(9,4^{-})
,
v_{1}
is
(\Delta,7)
, and
v_{2}
is
(\Delta,6)
, where
\Delta\in[11,13]
;

(

)

u
is
(9,4^{-})
,
v_{1}
is
(14,7)
, and
v_{2}
is
(13,7)
or
(14,7^{-})
;

(
A_{3.6}
)

u
is
(9,5)
and
v_{2}
is
([9,13],4^{-})
;

(

)

u
is
(9,5)
,
v_{1}
is
(\Delta,7)
, and

(\Delta,5)
, where
\Delta\in[10,11]
;

(

)

u
is
(9,5)
,
v_{1}
is
(\Delta,7)
, and
v_{2}
is
(\Delta,5/6)
, where
\Delta\in[12,13]
.

(
A_{4}
)

A
(10,5^{-})
vertex
u
adjacent to a
3
-vertex
v
with
N(v)=\{u,v_{1},v_{2}\}
and
uv_{1},uv_{2},v_{1}v_{2}\in E(G)
such that at least one of the following holds:

(
A_{4.1}
)

v_{2}
is
(d(v_{2}),4^{-})
;

(
A_{4.2}
)

v_{2}
is
(9,6)
or
([10,13],5/6)
, and
n_{6^{+}}(v_{1})\leq\Delta-d(v_{2})+6
;

(
A_{4.3}
)

is

and

;

(

)

is

, and

(\Delta,5)
, where

.

(

)

A

u
adjacent to a
3
-vertex
v
with
N(v)=\{u,v_{1},v_{2}\}
and
uv_{1},uv_{2},v_{1}v_{2}\in E(G)
such that at least one of the following holds:

(
A_{5.1}
)

v_{1}
is
([9,14],6)
or

(

(11,5^{-})
.

(

A

(

)

;

(
A_{6.2}
)

d(v)=6
,

;

(

)

,

(

)

,

,

,

, and

.

(

)

A

,

such that at least one of the following holds:

(

)

;

(

)

d(u_{3})=7
,

, and

;

(

)

.

(

)

-vertex

and

,

uvu_{4}

(

)

,

, and
\min\{d(u_{1}),d(u_{2})\}\leq 7
;

(
A_{8.2}
)

d(u_{3})\leq 6
,
u_{4}
is
(7,4^{-})
, and
\min\{d(u_{1}),d(u_{2})\}\leq 6
;

(
A_{8.3}
)

d(u_{3})\leq 6
,
u_{4}
is
(7,4^{-})
, and
d(u_{i})\leq 7
for each

;

(
A_{8.4}
)

u_{i}
is
(7,4^{-})
for each

and
d(u_{1})\leq 6
,
d(u_{2})\leq 7
.

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
2
, by contradiction built on discharging methods.
To this purpose, we assume to the contrary that
G
contains none of the local structures (
A_{1}
)–(
A_{8}
).

2.1
Definitions and notations

Since
G
is
2
-connected,
d(v)\geq 2
for every vertex
v\in V(G)
.
Let
G^{\prime}
be the graph obtained by deleting all the
2
-vertices of
G
, and let
H
be a connected component of
G^{\prime}
.
One sees that every vertex
v\in V(H)
has its (called
original
) degree
d(v)\geq 3
in
G
, and
H
is clearly also planar.
In what follows, we assume that
H
is embedded in the two dimensional plane such that its edges intersect only at their ending vertices,
and we refer
H
to as a
plane
graph.

For objects in the plane graph
H
, we use the following notations:

•

N_{H}(v)=\{u\mid uv\in E(H)\}
and
d_{H}(v)=|N_{H}(v)|
— the degree of the vertex
v\in V(H)
;

•

similarly define what a
k
-
vertex
, a
k^{+}
-
vertex
, and a
k^{-}
-
vertex
are;

•

n^{\prime}_{k}(v)
(
n^{\prime}_{k^{+}}(v)
,
n^{\prime}_{k^{-}}(v)
, respectively) — the number of
k
- (
k^{+}
-,
k^{-}
-, respectively) neighbors of
v
;

•

an
(S_{1},S_{2})_{H}
vertex — a vertex
x
with
d_{H}(x)\in S_{1}
and
n^{\prime}_{6^{+}}(x)\in S_{2}
;

•

an
(S_{1},S_{2})_{HG}
vertex — a vertex
x
with
d_{H}(x)=d(x)\in S_{1}
and
n^{\prime}_{6^{+}}(x)=n_{6^{+}}(x)\in S_{2}
.

Note that we do not discuss the embedding of the graph
G
.
Every face in the proof of Theorem
2
is in the embedding of the graph
H
, or is simplified as in the embedding of the graph
H
.
We reserve the character
f
to denote a face in the embedding.

•

F(H)
— the face set of
H
;

•

V_{k}(H)
— the set of
k
-vertices in
V(H)
;

•

V(f)
— the set of vertices on (the boundary of) the face
f
;

•

a vertex
v
is
incident with
a face
f
if
v\in V(f)
;

•

an edge
uv
is
incident with
a face
f
if
u,v\in V(f)
;

•

n_{k}(f)
(
n_{k^{+}}(f)
,
n_{k^{-}}(f)
, respectively) — the number of
k
- (
k^{+}
-,
k^{-}
-, respectively) vertices in
V(f)
;

•

\delta(f)
— the minimum degree of the vertices in
V(f)
;

•

d(f)
— the degree of the face
f
, which is the number of edges on the face
f
with cut-edges counted twice,
and similarly define what a
k
-
face
, a
k^{+}
-
face
and a
k^{-}
-
face
are;

•

F(v)=\{f\in F(H)\mid v\in V(f)\}
— the set of faces incident with
v
;

•

F_{3}(v)=\{f\in F(H)\mid v\in V(f),d(f)=3\}
— the set of
3
-faces incident with
v
;

•

F(uv)=\{f\in F(H)\mid uv\in E(G),u,v\in V(f)\}
— the set of faces incident with the edge
uv
;
note that
|F(uv)|=2
for any edge
uv
;

•

f_{uv}=F(uv)\setminus\{f\}
— the face incident with the edge
uv
and adjacent to the face
f\in F(uv)
;

•

m_{k}(v)
(
m_{k^{+}}(v)
,
m_{k^{-}}(v)
, respectively) — the number of
k
- (
k^{+}
-,
k^{-}
-, respectively) faces in
F(v)
;

•

m_{3}(uv)
— the number of
3
-faces in
F(uv)
.

2.2
Structural properties

Note from the construction of
H
that
d_{H}(u)=d(u)-n_{2}(u)
and thus
d_{H}(u)=d(u)
if
n_{2}(u)=0
.
In this subsection, we characterize structural properties for
5^{-}
-vertices in
H
, which are used in the discharging.

Lemma 1

For any
u\in V(H)
,

(1)

if
3\leq d(u)\leq 7
, then
d_{H}(u)=d(u)
;

(2)

if
d(u)\geq 8
, then
d_{H}(u)\geq 7
;

(3)

\delta(H)\geq 3
,
n^{\prime}_{2}(u)=0
,
n^{\prime}_{k}(u)=n_{k}(u)
,
k\in[3,6]
, and
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)=n_{5^{-}}(u)-n_{2}(u)
,
n^{\prime}_{6^{+}}(u)=n_{6^{+}}(u)
;

(4)

if
d(u)\geq 8
, then
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq d_{H}(u)-7
and
n^{\prime}_{6^{+}}(u)\geq 7
.

Proof.

The non-existence of (
A_{1.1}
) in
G
states that
uw\in E(G)
for each
2
-vertex
v
with
N(v)=\{u,w\}
.

The non-existence of (
A_{1.2}
) in
G
states that no
7^{-}
-vertex is adjacent to any
2
-vertex in
G
;
thus
3\leq d(u)\leq 7
implies
d_{H}(u)=d(u)
.

The non-existences of
(A_{1.3})
in
G
implies that every
8^{+}
-vertex
u
is adjacent to at most (
d(u)-7
)
2
-vertices in
G
;
thus if
d(u)\geq 8
, then
d_{H}(u)=d(u)-n_{2}(u)\geq d(u)-(d(u)-7)=7
.

Note that all
2
-vertices are deleted from
G
, a
k
-neighbor of
u
in
G
remains as a
k
-neighbor of
u
in
H
,
k\in[3,6]
,
and a
7^{+}
-neighbor of
u
in
G
remains as a
7^{+}
-neighbor of
u
in
H
,
Therefore,
\delta(H)\geq 3
,
n^{\prime}_{2}(u)=0
,
n^{\prime}_{k}(u)=n_{k}(u)
, for any
k\in[3,6]
, and
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)=n_{5^{-}}(u)-n_{2}(u)
,
n^{\prime}_{6^{+}}(u)=n_{6^{+}}(u)
.

The non-existences of
(A_{1.3})
in
G
implies that every
8^{+}
-vertex
u
is adjacent to at most (
d(u)-6
)
5^{-}
-vertices in
G
;
thus if
d(u)\geq 8
, then
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)=n_{5^{-}}(u)-n_{2}(u)\leq d(u)-7-(d(u)-d_{H}(u))=d_{H}(u)-7
, and
n^{\prime}_{6^{+}}(u)\geq 7
.

\Box

By Lemma
1
, for any
k
-vertex, where
k\in[3,6]
, we do not distinguish which graph,
G
or
H
, it is in;
furthermore, we use
d(u)
instead of
d_{H}(u)
, use
n_{k}(u)
instead of
n^{\prime}_{k}(u)
, and use
n_{6^{+}}(u)
instead of
n^{\prime}_{6^{+}}(u)
.

Corollary 1

For any vertex
u\in V(H)
, if
d(u)>d_{H}(u)\geq 7
, then
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq d_{H}(u)-7
,
n_{6^{+}}(u)=n^{\prime}_{6^{+}}(u)\geq 7
;
and if
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)=n_{5^{-}}(u)>0
, then
d_{H}(u)\geq 7
.

Lemma 2

For any
uv\in E(H)
,

(1)

if
d(v)=3
with
N(v)=\{u,v_{1},v_{2}\}
, then
d(u)\geq 8
and
d_{H}(u)\geq 8
;

(2)

if
d(u)=4
with
N(u)=\{v,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}\}
, then for each
i=1,2,3
, we have
d_{H}(u_{i})\geq 8
and

(2.1)

d(u_{i})\geq 10
if
d(v)=4
;

(2.2)

d(u_{i})\geq 9
if
d(v)=5
;

(2.3)

d(u_{i})\geq 8
if
d(v)=6
and
uv
is contained in a
3
-cycle;

(2.4)

d(u_{i})\geq 10
if
d(v)=5
and
uv
is contained in two
3
-cycles;

(2.5)

d(u_{i})\geq 9
if
d(v)=6
and
uv
is contained in two
3
-cycles;

(2.6)

d(u_{i})\geq 9
if
v
is
(7,4^{-})
and
uv
is contained in two
3
-cycles
uu_{2}v
,
uu_{3}v
with
u_{1}u_{2},u_{1}u_{3}\in E(G)
.

Proof.

The non-existence of (
A_{2.1}
) in
G
states that no
7^{-}
-vertex is adjacent to a
3
-vertex in
G
;
thus
d(u)\geq 8
and by Corollary
1
,
d_{H}(u)\geq 8
.
The non-existence of (
A_{1.1}
), (
A_{1.2}
), (
A_{2.1}
) and (
A_{6}
) in
G
and Corollary
1
imply that (2.1)-(2.6) hold.

\Box

Lemma 3

Let
u
,
v
,
u_{1}
,
v_{1}
be four
4^{-}
-vertices in
H
.

(1)

If
d(u)=3
and
d(v)\leq 4
, or
d(u)=d(v)=4
with
uv\not\in E(H)
,
then
F_{3}(u)\cap F_{3}(v)=\emptyset
.

(2)

If
d(u)=d(v)=4
with
uv\in E(H)
and
d(u_{1})\leq 4
,
then
(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))\cap F_{3}(u_{1})=\emptyset
.

(3)

If
d(u)=d(v)=4
with
uv\in E(H)
and
d(u_{1})=d(v_{1})=4
with
u_{1}v_{1}\in E(H)
,
then
(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))\cap(F_{3}(u_{1})\cup F_{3}(v_{1}))=\emptyset
.
\Box

2.3
Discharging to show contradictions

To derive a contradiction, we make use of the discharging methods, which are very similar to an amortized analysis through token re-distribution.
First, by Euler’s formula on the embedding
|V(H)|-|E(H)|+|F(H)|=2
and the relation
\sum_{v\in V(H)}d_{H}(v)=\sum_{f\in F(H)}d(f)=2|E(H)|
,
we have the following equality:

\sum_{u\in V(H)}(4-d_{H}(u))+\sum_{f\in F(H)}(4-d(f))=8.

(1)

Next, we define an initial
weight
(i.e.,
token
, or
charge
) function
\omega(\cdot)
on
V(H)\cup F(H)
by
setting
\omega(u)=4-d_{H}(u)
for each vertex
u\in V(H)
and
setting
\omega(f)=4-d(f)
for each face
f\in F(H)
.
It follows from Eq. (
1
) that the total weight is equal to
8
.
We define next a set of discharging rules (R1)–(R5) to move portions of weights from
x
to
z
(possibly through
y
), that is,
the function
\tau(x\rightarrow z)
(or
\tau(x\rightarrow z)_{y}
), where
x,y,z\in V(H)\cup F(H)
.
The effect of this function decreases the charge at
x
by
\tau(x\rightarrow z)_{y}
, leaves the charge of
y
unchanged, and increases the charge of
z
by
\tau(x\rightarrow z)_{y}
.
For counting purposes, we say that
x
sends the charge to
y
, and
z
receives the charge from
y
.
At the end of the discharging, a new weight function
\omega^{\prime}(\cdot)
is achieved and we will show that
\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 0
for every element
x\in V(H)\cup F(H)
.
This contradicts the positive total weight.

Before presenting the discharging rules, we introduce an important parameter
\alpha_{u}
for a vertex
u\in V(H)
satisfying
d_{H}(u)=k\geq 6
and
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)>0
:

\alpha_{u}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{k-4-\frac{1}{3}(k-2n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u))}{2n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)}=\frac{k-6}{3n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)}+\frac{1}{3},&\mbox{ if }n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor;\\
\frac{k-4}{k}=1-\frac{4}{k},&\mbox{ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(2)

The following properties hold for
\alpha_{u}
, of which some values are given in Figure
2
.

Lemma 4

Let
d_{H}(u)=k\geq 6
with
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)>0
.

(1)

\alpha_{u}\geq 1-\frac{4}{k}
.

(2)

If
d_{H}(u)=6
, then
\alpha_{u}=\frac{1}{3}
;
if
d_{H}(u)\geq 12
, then
\alpha_{u}\geq\frac{2}{3}
.

(3)

If
u
is
(k,7^{+})_{H}
, then
(i)
if
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor
, then
\alpha_{u}\geq\frac{2}{3}+\frac{1}{3n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)}>\frac{2}{3}
;

(ii)

\alpha_{u}\geq\frac{5}{7}>\frac{2}{3}
.

(4)

If
d(u)>d_{H}(u)\geq 8
, then
\alpha_{u}\geq\frac{5}{7}>\frac{2}{3}
.

(5)

If
u
is
(k,6^{+})_{H}
, then
\alpha_{u}\geq\frac{2}{3}
.

(6)

If
\alpha_{u}<\frac{2}{3}
, then
d(u)=d_{H}(u)=k\in[7,11]
,
u
is
(k,5^{-})
, and if
n^{\prime}_{3}(u)>0
, then
k\in[8,11]
.

Proof.

By the definition of
\alpha_{u}
in Eq. (
2
), to prove (1) it suffices to assume that
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor
.
In this case, we have
\alpha_{u}-(1-\frac{4}{k})=\frac{k-6}{3n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)}-\frac{2}{3}+\frac{4}{k}\geq\frac{k-6}{3\times\frac{k}{2}}-\frac{2}{3}+\frac{4}{k}=0
,
i.e.,
\alpha_{u}\geq 1-\frac{4}{k}
.

For (2), if
d_{H}(u)=6
, then by the definition
\alpha_{u}=\frac{1}{3}
;
if
d_{H}(u)\geq 12
, then by (1)
\alpha_{u}\geq\frac{2}{3}
.

To prove (3), if
d_{H}(u)\geq 14
, then by (1),
\alpha_{u}\geq 1-\frac{4}{k}\geq\frac{5}{7}
.
If
d_{H}(u)\leq 14
, then
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq k-7\leq\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor\leq 7
;
thus,
\alpha_{u}=\frac{k-6}{3n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)}+\frac{1}{3}\geq\frac{1}{3n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)}+\frac{2}{3}\geq\frac{5}{7}
.

By Corollary
1
, if
d(u)>d_{H}(u)
, then we have
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq d_{H}(u)-7
and thus
u
is
(k,7^{+})_{H}
.
It follows from (3) that
\alpha_{u}\geq\frac{5}{7}>\frac{2}{3}
.
This proves (4).

To prove (5), if
d_{H}(u)\geq 12
, then by (2)
\alpha_{u}\geq\frac{2}{3}
.
If
d_{H}(u)\leq 11
, then
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq k-6\leq\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor
;
thus,
\alpha_{u}=\frac{k-6}{3n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)}+\frac{1}{3}\geq\frac{k-6}{3(k-6)}+\frac{1}{3}=\frac{2}{3}.

Lastly, if
\alpha_{u}<\frac{2}{3}
, then by (2) and (5)
d(u)=d_{H}(u)=k\in[7,11]
and
u
is
(k,5^{-})
.
If
n^{\prime}_{3}(u)>0
, then by the non-existence of (
A_{2.1}
) in
G
, we have
k\in[8,11]
.
This proves (6).

\Box

Figure 2:
The
\alpha_{u}
value for a vertex
u
with
d_{H}(u)=k\in[7,15]
and
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq 9
.

We now present the discharging rules (R1)–(R5).
A graphical view of how weights are moved from an element to another is depicted in Figure
3
.

(R1)

For each
4^{-}
-vertex
u
adjacent to a
6^{+}
-vertex
v
with
F(uv)=\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
,
if
d(f_{1})\geq 4
and
d(f_{2})\geq 4
, then
\tau(u\rightarrow v)_{f_{1}}=\tau(u\rightarrow v)_{f_{2}}=\frac{\alpha_{v}}{2}
.

In the other four rules (R2)–(R5),
f=[uvw]
is a
3
-face.

(R2)

If
\delta(f)\geq 6
, then for any
x\in V(f)
,
\tau(f\rightarrow x)=\frac{1}{3}
.

In the rest three rules (R3)–(R5),
\delta(f)\leq 5
and
d(u)\leq 5
.

(R3)

For any
x\in\{v,w\}
with
d_{H}(x)\geq 6
,

(R3.1)

\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f}=\alpha_{x}
;

(R3.2)

\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f_{ux}}=\begin{cases}\frac{\alpha_{x}}{2},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{ux})\geq 4;\\
0,&\mbox{ if }d(f_{ux})=3.\end{cases}

(R4)

If
d(u)=4
,
d(v)=5
and
m_{3}(u)=4
with
f_{uv}=[uvw^{*}]
, then

(R4.1)

\tau(F(uv)\rightarrow v)=\frac{1}{10}
if
\min\{d(f_{vw}),d((f_{uv})_{vw^{*}})\}=3
and
\max\{d(f_{vw}),d((f_{uv})_{vw^{*}})\}\geq 4
;

(R4.2)

\tau(F(uv)\rightarrow v)=\frac{2}{5}
if
d(f_{vw})=d((f_{uv})_{vw^{*}})=3
.

(R5)

If
\delta(f)=d(u)=5
with
n_{5}(f)=k
, then

(R5.1)

if after applying (R3), the face
f
still has charge
\beta\geq 0
, then
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\frac{\beta}{k}
;

(R5.2)

if
d_{H}(v)\geq 6
,
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
, and
\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}\geq 1
, then
\tau(u\rightarrow f)=\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}-1
.

Figure 3:
The discharging rules (R1)–(R5): A graphical view of how weights are moved from an element to another by red arrows,
each is labeled with the weight.

In the rest of this section we prove that at the end of discharging the new weight function
\omega^{\prime}(\cdot)\leq 0
, through ten Discharging-Lemmas.
The first of which is obvious.

Discharging-Lemma 1

For every face
f
, if
d(f)\geq 4
, then
f
does not receive or give any weight and thus
\omega^{\prime}(f)=\omega(f)=4-d(f)\leq 0
.

Proof.

This holds trivially since the discharging rules (R1)–(R5) do not apply to
f
with
d(f)\geq 4
.

\Box

Discharging-Lemma 2

For every vertex
v\in V(H)
with
d_{H}(v)=k\geq 6
,
\omega^{\prime}(v)\leq 0
.

Proof.

Let
f=[\ldots uvw\ldots]
be a face incident to
v
.
If
d_{H}(u)\geq 6
and
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
, then by (R2),
\tau(f\rightarrow v)\leq\frac{1}{3}
.
If
d_{H}(u)\leq 5
and
d(f)=3
, then by (R3.1),
\tau(f\rightarrow v)_{f}=\alpha_{v}
.
If
d_{H}(u)\leq 5
and
d(f)\geq 4
, then by (R1), (R3.1) and (R3.2),
\tau(u\rightarrow v)_{f}+\tau(f_{vu}\rightarrow v)_{f}=\frac{\alpha_{v}}{2}
,
and
\tau(w\rightarrow v)_{f}+\tau(f_{vw}\rightarrow v)_{f}\leq\frac{\alpha_{v}}{2}
.

Let
k_{1}
be the number of faces
f=[\ldots uvw\ldots]
incident to
v
such that
d_{H}(u)\geq 6
and
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
.
The total weight transferred to
v
is at most
k_{1}\times\frac{1}{3}+(d_{H}(v)-k_{1})\times\alpha_{v}
.
It follows that

\omega^{\prime}(v)\leq\omega(v)+k_{1}\times\frac{1}{3}+(d_{H}(v)-k_{1})\times\alpha_{v}=4-d_{H}(v)+k_{1}\times\frac{1}{3}+(d_{H}(v)-k_{1})\times\alpha_{v}=4-k+k\times\alpha_{v}-(\alpha_{v}-\frac{1}{3})\times k_{1}
.

If
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\geq\lceil\frac{k}{2}\rceil
,
then
\omega^{\prime}(v)\leq 4-k+k\times\alpha_{v}=4-k+k\times\frac{k-4}{k}=0
.
If
n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)\leq\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor
, then it follows from
k_{1}\geq k-2n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u)
that

\omega^{\prime}(v)\leq 4-k+k\times\alpha_{v}-(\alpha_{v}-\frac{1}{3})\times(k-2n^{\prime}_{5^{-}}(u))=4-\frac{2}{3}\times k+\frac{2}{3}\times(k-6)=0
.

\Box

Discharging-Lemma 3

For every vertex
v\in V(H)
with
d(v)=3
,
\sum_{x\in\{v\}\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 0
.

Proof.

Let
u_{i}v\in E(H)
, and
f_{i}=[\ldots u_{i}vu_{(i\bmod 3)+1}\ldots]
,
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, be the three faces incident to
v
.
One sees from Lemma
2
(1) that
d(u_{i})\geq 8
,

d_{H}(u_{i})\geq 8
and
\alpha_{u_{i}}\geq\frac{1}{2}
for each
i=1,2,3
.
We distinguish the following four cases for
m_{3}(v)
.

Case 1.

m_{3}(v)=0
.
In this case, by (R1),
\tau(v\rightarrow u_{1})_{f_{3}}+\tau(v\rightarrow u_{1})_{f_{1}}=\alpha_{u_{1}}
,

\tau(v\rightarrow u_{2})_{f_{1}}+\tau(v\rightarrow u_{2})_{f_{2}}=\alpha_{u_{2}}
, and

\tau(v\rightarrow u_{3})_{f_{2}}+\tau(v\rightarrow u_{3})_{f_{3}}=\alpha_{u_{3}}
.
Thus,

\sum_{x\in\{v\}\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)=\omega^{\prime}(v)=\omega(v)-\sum_{i\in[1,3]}\tau(v\rightarrow u_{i})=1-\sum_{i\in[1,3]}\alpha_{u_{i}}\leq 1-3\times\frac{1}{2}<0.

Case 2.

m_{3}(v)=1
with
d(f_{2})=3
.
In this case, by (R1), (R3.1) and (R3.2),
\tau(v\rightarrow u_{1})_{f_{3}}+\tau(v\rightarrow u_{1})_{f_{1}}=\alpha_{u_{1}}
,

\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{2})_{f_{1}}=\frac{\alpha_{u_{2}}}{2}
,

\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{3})_{f_{3}}=\frac{\alpha_{u_{3}}}{2}
, and
for each
i\in[2,3]
,
\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{i})_{f_{2}}=\alpha_{u_{i}}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\{v\}\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\{v,f_{2}\}}\omega(x)-\left(\tau(v\rightarrow u_{1})+\sum_{i\in[2,3]}\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{i})+\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{2})_{f_{1}}+\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{3})_{f_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\{v,f_{2}\}}(4-3)-(\alpha_{u_{1}}+\alpha_{u_{2}}+\alpha_{u_{3}}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{u_{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{u_{3}})\leq 2\times 1-4\times\frac{1}{2}=0.

Case 3.

m_{3}(v)=2
with
d(f_{2})=d(f_{3})=3
.
In this case, by (R3.1) and (R3.2),
\tau(f_{3}\rightarrow u_{1})_{f_{3}}=\alpha_{u_{1}}
,

\tau(f_{3}\rightarrow u_{1})_{f_{1}}=\frac{\alpha_{u_{1}}}{2}
,

\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{2})_{f_{2}}=\alpha_{u_{2}}
,

\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{2})_{f_{1}}=\frac{\alpha_{u_{2}}}{2}
,
and
\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{3})_{f_{2}}=\tau(f_{3}\rightarrow u_{3})_{f_{3}}=\alpha_{u_{3}}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\{v\}\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\{v,f_{2},f_{3}\}}\omega(x)-\left(\sum_{i\in[2,3]}\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{i})+\sum_{i\in\{1,3\}}\tau(f_{3}\rightarrow u_{i})+\tau(f_{3}\rightarrow u_{1})_{f_{1}}+\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u_{2})_{f_{1}}\right)

(3)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\{v,f_{2},f_{3}\}}(4-3)-(\alpha_{u_{2}}+\alpha_{u_{3}}+\alpha_{u_{1}}+\alpha_{u_{3}}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{u_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{u_{2}})

\displaystyle=3\times 1-(2\alpha_{u_{3}}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{1}}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{2}}).

Below we distinguish two cases where
u_{1}u_{2}\in E(G)
and
u_{1}u_{2}\not\in E(G)
, respectively.

Case 3.1.
u_{1}u_{2}\in E(G)
.
In this case, for each
i\in[1,3]
, the non-existence of (
A_{2.1}
) and (
A_{2.4}
) in
G
states that
d(u_{i})\geq 9
;
by Corollary
1
, no
7
-vertex is adjacent to any
5^{-}
-vertex in
H
and
d_{H}(u_{i})\geq 8
.
Thus, we have
\alpha_{u_{i}}\geq\frac{5}{9}
for each
i\in[1,3]
.
Note that if
d_{H}(u_{i})=8
for some
i\in[1,3]
, then
d(u_{i})\geq 9>d_{H}(u_{i})
, and it follows from Lemma
4
that
\alpha_{u_{i}}\geq\frac{5}{7}>\frac{2}{3}
.

When
\alpha_{u_{3}}\geq\frac{2}{3}
, Eq. (
3
) becomes

\sum_{x\in\{v\}\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 3-(2\times\frac{2}{3}+\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{5}{9}+\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{5}{9})=0
.

When
\alpha_{u_{1}}\geq\frac{7}{11}
and
\alpha_{u_{2}}\geq\frac{7}{11}
, Eq. (
3
) becomes

\sum_{x\in\{v\}\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 3-(2\times\frac{5}{9}+\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{7}{11}+\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{7}{11})=-\frac{1}{9}+\frac{1}{11}<0
.

When
\alpha_{u_{3}}<\frac{2}{3}
and
\alpha_{u_{i}}<\frac{7}{11}
for some
i\in[1,2]
,
it follows from Lemma
4
that
d_{H}(u_{3})=d(u_{3})\in[9,11]
,
u_{3}
is
(d(u_{3}),5^{-})
,
and there exists an
i\in[1,2]
such that
d_{H}(u_{i})=d(u_{i})\in[9,10]
,
u_{i}
is
(d(u_{i}),5^{-})
.
It follows that there exists a
w\in N(v)
such that
w
is
(10,5^{-})
.

Assume that
w_{1}
is
(9,5^{-})
.
The non-existence of (
A_{3.3}
) in
G
implies that
w_{1}
is
(9,5)
;
the non-existence of (
A_{3.6}
) in
G
implies that there exists a
w_{2}\in N(v)\setminus\{w_{1}\}
such that
w_{2}
is
([9,11],5)
.
Let
w_{3}\in N(v)\setminus\{w_{1},w_{2}\}
.
Then by the non-existence of (
A_{3.2}
) in
G
, we have
d_{H}(w_{3})=d(w_{3})=\Delta
and
w_{3}
is
(\Delta,7^{+})_{HG}
.
It is clear that
w_{3}\in\{u_{1},u_{2}\}
, i.e.,
w_{3}\neq u_{3}
.
Therefore,
\alpha_{w_{1}}\geq\frac{7}{12}
,
\alpha_{w_{2}}\geq\frac{7}{12}
, and by Lemma
4

\alpha_{w_{3}}\geq\frac{5}{7}
.
Hence, Eq. (
3
) becomes

\sum_{x\in\{v\}\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 3-(2\times\frac{7}{12}+\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{7}{12}+\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{5}{7})=-\frac{1}{24}-\frac{1}{14}<0
.

Assume that there is no
(9,5^{-})
in
\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}\}
and
w_{1}
is
(10,5^{-})
.
The non-existence of (
A_{4.1}
) in
G
implies that there exists a
w_{2}\in N(v)\setminus\{w_{1}\}
such that
w_{2}
is
(10/11,5)
.
Let
w_{3}\in N(v)\setminus\{w_{1},w_{2}\}
.
Thus, by the non-existence of (
A_{4.3}
) in
G
, we have
d_{H}(w_{3})=d(w_{3})=\Delta
and by the non-existence of (
A_{4.2}
) in
G
,
w_{3}
is
(\Delta,7^{+})_{HG}
.
It is clear that
w_{3}\in\{u_{1},u_{2}\}
, i.e.,
w_{3}\neq u_{3}
.
Therefore,
\alpha_{w_{1}}\geq\frac{3}{5}
,
\alpha_{w_{2}}\geq\frac{3}{5}
, and by Lemma
4

\alpha_{w_{3}}\geq\frac{5}{7}
.
Hence, Eq. (
3
) becomes

\sum_{x\in\{v\}\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 3-(2\times\frac{3}{5}+\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{3}{5}+\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{5}{7})=-\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{14}<0
.

Case 3.2.
u_{1}u_{2}\not\in E(G)
.

, or

u_{3}

, or

(4)

\displaystyle=4-2(\alpha_{u_{1}}+\alpha_{u_{2}}+\alpha_{u_{3}}).

\Box

Discharging-Lemma 4

For every vertex
u\in V(H)
with
d_{H}(u)=d(u)=4
, we have

(1)

for each
6^{+}
-vertex
x
in
N_{H}(u)
, the total weight transferred from
u
and the
3
-faces in
F(ux)
to
x
is
(1+\frac{1}{2}m_{3}(ux))\alpha_{x}
;

(2)

if
u
is adjacent to some
4
-vertex
v
in
H
, then
\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 0
;

(3)

if
d_{H}(v)\geq 5
for each
v\in N_{H}(u)
, then
\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 0
.

Proof.

For each
x\in N_{H}(u)
with
d_{H}(x)\geq 6
, let
\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)
be the total weight transferred from
u
and the
3
-faces in
F(ux)
to
x
.
To prove (1), we discuss the following three subcases for the three possible values of
m_{3}(ux)
.

•

m_{3}(ux)=0
. By (R1),
\tau(u\rightarrow x)_{f}=\tau(u\rightarrow x)_{f_{ux}}=\frac{1}{2}\times\alpha_{x}
;
and thus
\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)=\tau(u\rightarrow x)_{f}+\tau(u\rightarrow x)_{f_{ux}}=\alpha_{x}
.

•

m_{3}(ux)=1
with
d(f_{3})=3
. By (R3.1) and (R3.2),
\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f}=\alpha_{x}
,
\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f_{ux}}=\frac{1}{2}\times\alpha_{x}
;
and thus
\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)=\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f}+\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f_{ux}}=\alpha_{x}+\frac{1}{2}\times\alpha_{x}=\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{x}
.

•

m_{3}(ux)=2
. By (R3.1),
\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f}=\tau(f_{ux}\rightarrow x)_{f_{ux}}=\alpha_{x}
;
and thus
\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)=\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f}+\tau(f_{ux}\rightarrow x)_{f_{ux}}=\alpha_{x}+\alpha_{x}=2\alpha_{x}
.

Hence, the total weight transferred from
u
and the
3
-faces in
F(ux)
to
x
is
(1+\frac{1}{2}m_{3}(ux))\alpha_{x}
.

Now let
u_{1},u_{2},v,u_{3}
be all the neighbors of
u
in clockwise order,
and denote the face containing
u_{1}uu_{2}
(
u_{2}uv
,
vuu_{3}
,

, respectively) as

(
f_{2},f_{3},f_{4}
, respectively).
Note that

\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega(x)=\sum_{x\in F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega(x)=m_{3}(u)+m_{3}(v)-m_{3}(uv).

(5)

To prove (2), we have
d(v)=d_{H}(v)=4
and let
v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}
be the other neighbors of
v
in clockwise order,
and denote the face containing

(

(

, respectively).

For each

2

and

and then by Lemma

\alpha_{x}\geq\frac{3}{5}
.
Using (R1), (R3.1) and (R3.1), we discuss the following three subcases for the three possible values of
m_{3}(uv)
.

Case 1.
m_{3}(uv)=0
.
In this case,
m_{3}(u)\leq 2
and
m_{3}(v)\leq 2
.
It follows from Eq. (
5
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))}\omega^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))}\omega(x)-\sum_{x\in\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}\}}\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)

(6)

\displaystyle=m_{3}(u)+m_{3}(v)-\sum_{x\in\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}\}}\Gamma(u\rightarrow x).

By (1),
\sum_{x\in N_{H}(u)\setminus\{v\}}\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)\geq\alpha_{u_{1}}+\alpha_{u_{2}}+\alpha_{u_{3}}
,

\sum_{x\in N_{H}(v)\setminus\{u\}}\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)\geq\alpha_{v_{1}}+\alpha_{v_{2}}+\alpha_{v_{3}}
.

When

, Eq. (
6
) becomes

\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 3-(\alpha_{u_{1}}+\alpha_{u_{2}}+\alpha_{u_{3}}+\alpha_{v_{1}}+\alpha_{v_{2}}+\alpha_{v_{3}})\leq 3-6\times\frac{3}{5}=-\frac{3}{5}<0
.

In the other case,
m_{3}(u)+m_{3}(v)=4
, i.e.,
m_{3}(u)=m_{3}(v)=2
.
Then by (1),
\sum_{x\in N_{H}(u)\setminus\{v\}}\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)\geq 2\alpha_{u_{1}}+\alpha_{u_{2}}+\alpha_{u_{3}}
,

, and Eq. (
6
) becomes

\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 4-(2\alpha_{u_{1}}+\alpha_{u_{2}}+\alpha_{u_{3}}+\alpha_{v_{1}}+2\alpha_{v_{2}}+\alpha_{v_{3}})\leq 4-8\times\frac{3}{5}=-\frac{4}{5}<0
.

Case 2.
m_{3}(uv)=1
with

.

,

if

,

is a

,

.
That is,

\displaystyle\Gamma(u/v\rightarrow u_{2})=\begin{cases}3\alpha_{u_{2}},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{1})=d(h_{1})=3;\\
2\alpha_{u_{2}},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{1}),d(h_{1})\geq 4;\\
\frac{5}{2}\alpha_{u_{2}},&\mbox{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(7)

By (1) and Eq. (
5
),
\sum_{x\in\{u_{1},u_{3}\}}\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)\geq\alpha_{u_{1}}+\alpha_{u_{3}}
,

, and

(9)

By (1),
\Gamma(u\rightarrow u_{1})=(1+\frac{1}{2}m_{3}(uu_{1}))\alpha_{u_{1}}
,

, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))}\omega^{\prime}(x)=m_{3}(u)+m_{3}(v)-2-\sum_{x\in\{u_{1},v_{2}\}}\Gamma(u\rightarrow x)-\sum_{y\in\{u_{2},u_{3}\}}\Gamma(u/v\rightarrow y)

(10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{u_{1}})\times m_{3}(uu_{1})+(1-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{v_{2}})\times m_{3}(vv_{2})+\alpha_{u_{1}}+\alpha_{v_{2}}+2-\sum_{y\in\{u_{2},u_{3}\}}\Gamma(u/v\rightarrow y)

When

, Eq. (

) becomes

\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq(1-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{u_{1}})\times m_{3}(uu_{1})+(1-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{v_{2}})\times m_{3}(vv_{2})+2-\alpha_{u_{1}}-\alpha_{v_{2}}-2\alpha_{u_{2}}-2\alpha_{u_{3}}\leq(1-\frac{1}{2}\times\frac{3}{5})\times 2+2-6\times\frac{3}{5}=-\frac{1}{5}<0
.

In the other case,

.

Hence, Eq. (

) becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))}\omega^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{u_{1}})\times 2+(1-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{v_{2}})\times m_{3}(vv_{2})+2-\alpha_{u_{1}}-\alpha_{v_{2}}-3\alpha_{u_{2}}-(\frac{5}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\times(m_{3}(vv_{2})-1))\times\alpha_{u_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4-2\alpha_{u_{1}}-\alpha_{v_{2}}-3\alpha_{u_{2}}-2\alpha_{u_{3}}+(1-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{v_{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{u_{3}})m_{3}(vv_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4-8\times\frac{3}{5}+(1-\frac{3}{5})m_{3}(vv_{2})=-\frac{4}{5}+(1-\frac{3}{5})m_{3}(vv_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{5}+\frac{2}{5}\times 2=0.

To prove (3), we have

\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega(x)=\omega(u)+\sum_{x\in F_{3}(u)}\omega(x)=\sum_{x\in F_{3}(u)}1=m_{3}(u)=m_{3}(uu_{1})+m_{3}(uv)=m_{3}(uu_{2})+m_{3}(uu_{3}).

Using (R1), (R3.1), (R3.2), (R4.1) and (R4.2), we discuss the following two cases for the minimum degree of vertices in
N_{H}(u)
.

Case 1. For each
x\in N_{H}(u)

(11)

11
) becomes
\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega^{\prime}(x)=2-(\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{1}}+2\alpha_{u_{2}}+\alpha_{u_{3}})\leq 2-\frac{9}{2}\times\frac{5}{9}=-\frac{1}{2}<0
.

•

d(f_{1})\geq 4
and
d(f_{4})=3
.
Then Eq. (
11
) becomes
\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega^{\prime}(x)=2-(\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{1}}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{2}}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{3}})\leq 2-\frac{9}{2}\times\frac{5}{9}<0
.

When
m_{3}(uv)=2
, i.e.,
d(f_{3})=d(f_{2})=3
, it follows from Lemma
2
(2.4) that for each
x\in\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}\}
,
d_{H}(x)\geq 8
and
d(x)\geq 10
;
and thus, by Lemma
4
(1) and (4),
\alpha_{x}\geq\frac{3}{5}
.
We discuss the following three subcases for three possible values of
m_{3}(u)
.

•

m_{3}(u)=2
, i.e.,
d(f_{1})\geq 4
and
d(f_{4})\geq 4
.
Then Eq. (
11
) becomes
\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega^{\prime}(x)=2-(\alpha_{u_{1}}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{2}}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{3}})\leq 2-4\times\frac{3}{5}=-\frac{2}{5}<0
.

•

m_{3}(u)=3
, and assuming w.l.o.g.
d(f_{1})=3
and
d(f_{4})\geq 4
.
Then Eq. (
11
) becomes
\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega^{\prime}(x)=3-(\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{1}}+2\alpha_{u_{2}}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{u_{3}})\leq 3-5\times\frac{3}{5}=0
.

•

m_{3}(u)=4
, i.e.,
d(f_{1})=d(f_{4})=3
.
We can have the following three scenarios for three possible values of
d((f_{2})_{u_{2}v})
and
d((f_{3})_{u_{3}v})
:

–

d((f_{2})_{u_{2}v})\geq 4
and
d((f_{3})_{u_{3}v})\geq 4
.
Then Eq. (
11
) becomes
\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega^{\prime}(x)=4-(2\alpha_{u_{1}}+\frac{5}{2}\alpha_{u_{2}}+\frac{5}{2}\alpha_{u_{3}})\leq 4-7\times\frac{3}{5}=-\frac{1}{5}<0
.

–

\min\{d((f_{2})_{u_{2}v}),d((f_{3})_{u_{3}v})\}=3
and
\max\{d(f_{vw}),d((f_{uv})_{vw^{*}})\}\geq 4
,
and assuming w.l.o.g. that
d((f_{2})_{u_{2}v})=3
and
d((f_{3})_{u_{3}v})\geq 4
.
Then by (R4.1),
\tau(F(uv)\rightarrow v)=\frac{1}{10}
, and thus Eq. (
11
) becomes

\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega^{\prime}(x)=4-(2\alpha_{u_{1}}+2\alpha_{u_{2}}+\frac{5}{2}\alpha_{u_{3}}+\frac{1}{10})\leq 4-\frac{13}{2}\times\frac{3}{5}-\frac{1}{10}=0
.

–

d((f_{2})_{u_{2}v})=3
and
d((f_{3})_{u_{3}v})=3
.
Then by (R4.2),
\tau(F(uv)\rightarrow v)=\frac{2}{5}
, and thus Eq. (
11
) becomes

\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega^{\prime}(x)=4-(2\alpha_{u_{1}}+2\alpha_{u_{2}}+2\alpha_{u_{3}}+\frac{2}{5})\leq 4-6\times\frac{3}{5}-\frac{2}{5}=0
.

This finishes the proof of the lemma.

\Box

Discharging-Lemma 5

Let
V_{34}=V_{3}(H)\cup V_{4}(H)
and
F_{0}=\bigcup_{v\in V_{34}(H)}F_{3}(v)
. Then

\sum_{x\in V_{34}\cup F_{0}}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 0.

Proof.

It holds naturally by Lemma
3
, Discharging-Lemmas
3
and
4
.

\Box

Discharging-Lemma 6

For every
3
-face
f=[uvw]\in F(H)
with
\delta(f)\geq 5
,
\omega^{\prime}(f)\leq 0
.

Proof.

When
\delta(f)\geq 6
, by (R2), we have for each
x\in V(f)
,
\tau(f\rightarrow v)_{f}=\frac{1}{3}
, and thus

\omega^{\prime}(f)=\omega(f)-\sum_{x\in\{u,v,w\}}\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f}=4-3-3\times\frac{1}{3}=0
.

When
\delta(f)=5
, i.e, there exists
x\in\{u,v,w\}
such that
d(x)=d_{H}(x)=5
, and assuming w.l.o.g.
d(u)=d_{H}(u)=5
.
If
d_{H}(v)\geq 6
,
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
, and
\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}\geq 1
,
then by (R3.1), (R3.2), and (R5.2),
\tau(f\rightarrow v)_{f}=\alpha_{v}
,
\tau(f\rightarrow w)_{f}=\alpha_{w}
,

\tau(u\rightarrow f)=\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}-1
,
and thus

\omega^{\prime}(f)=\omega(f)+\tau(u\rightarrow f)-\sum_{x\in\{v,w\}}\tau(f\rightarrow x)_{f}\leq 4-3+(\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}-1)-(\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w})=0
.
Otherwise, by (R3.1), (R3.2) and (R5.1), the total weight transferred from
f
to
u
,
v
,
w
is at least
1
,
and thus
\omega^{\prime}(f)\leq\omega(f)-1=4-3-1=0
.

\Box

One sees that for each
5
-vertex
x
in
H
,
\omega(x)=4-5=-1
, and
d(x)=d_{H}(x)=5
.
It remains to validate that
\omega^{\prime}(u)\leq 0
for every
u\in V(H)
with
d_{H}(u)=5
.

In the sequel we assume that
d(u)=d_{H}(u)=5
.
Before validating
\omega^{\prime}(u)
, using (R3.1), (R3.2), and (R5.1),
we characterize of the weight
\tau(f\rightarrow u)
transferred from an incident
3
-face
f=[uvw]
to
u
in Discharging-Lemma
7
,
also depicted in Figure
4
.

Figure 4:
The weight
\tau(f\rightarrow u)
transferred from an incident
3
-face
f=[uvw]
to
u
.

Discharging-Lemma 7

For every
5
-vertex
u
in
V(H)
,
let
f=[uvw]
be a
3
-face,
if
d_{H}(v)\geq 6
,
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
, and
\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}\geq 1
,
then
\tau(u\rightarrow f)=\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}-1
;
otherwise,
\tau(f\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{1}{3}
, with the following details:

(1)

if
d_{H}(v)=d_{H}(w)=5
, then
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\frac{1}{3}
;

(2)

if
d_{H}(v)=5
and
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
, then

\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\begin{cases}\frac{1-\alpha_{w}}{2},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uw})=d(f_{vw})=3,\mbox{ and }\alpha_{w}\leq 1;\\
\frac{1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}}{2},&\mbox{ if }3\in\{d(f_{uw}),d(f_{vw})\},\max\{d(f_{uw}),d(f_{vw})\}\geq 4,\mbox{ and }\alpha_{w}\leq\frac{2}{3};\\
\frac{1-2\alpha_{w}}{2},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uw})\geq 4,d(f_{vw})\geq 4,\mbox{ and }\alpha_{w}\leq\frac{1}{2};\end{cases}

(3)

if
d_{H}(v)\geq 6
, and
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
, then

\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\begin{cases}1-\alpha_{v}-\alpha_{w},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uv})=d(f_{uw})=3,\mbox{ and }\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}\leq 1;\\
1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}-\alpha_{w},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uv})\geq 4,d(f_{uw})=3,\mbox{ and }\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}\leq 1;\\
1-\alpha_{v}-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uv})=3,d(f_{uw})\geq 4,\mbox{ and }\alpha_{v}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}\leq 1;\\
1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}=0,&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uv})\geq 4,d(f_{uw})\geq 4,\mbox{ and }\alpha_{v}=\alpha_{w}=\frac{1}{3}.\end{cases}

Proof.

When
d_{H}(v)\geq 6
,
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
, and
\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}\geq 1
,
then it follows from (R5.2) clearly that
\tau(u\rightarrow f)=\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}-1
.
In the other case, either
\min\{d_{H}(v),d_{H}(w)\}=5
,
or
d_{H}(v)\geq 6
,
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
and
\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}<1
.
We assume that after applying (R3), the face
f
still has charge
\beta\geq 0
.
Using (R3.1), (R3.2), and (R5.1), we discuss the following three subcases for three possible values of
n_{5}(f)
.

Case 1.
n_{5}(f)=3
, i.e,
d_{H}(v)=d_{H}(w)=5
.
In this case, no weight transferred from
f
to
u
,
v
, or
w
by (R3.1) and (R3.2).
It follows that
\beta=1
, and
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\frac{\beta}{3}=\frac{1}{3}
.

Case 2.
n_{5}(f)=2
, and assuming w.l.o.g.
d_{H}(v)=5
and
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
.
In this case,
\tau(f\rightarrow w)_{f}=\alpha_{w}
and
\tau(f\rightarrow w)_{f_{uw}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{w}
,

\tau(f\rightarrow w)_{f_{vw}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{w}
.
Recall that
\alpha_{w}\geq\frac{1}{3}
.

•

d(f_{uw})=d(f_{vw})=3
.
It follows that
\beta=1-\alpha_{w}
.
If
\alpha_{w}\leq 1
, then
\beta\geq 0
and thus
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\frac{\beta}{2}=\frac{1-\alpha_{w}}{2}\leq\frac{1-\frac{1}{3}}{2}=\frac{1}{3}
.

•

3\in\{d(f_{uw}),d(f_{vw})\}
and
\max\{d(f_{uw}),d(f_{vw})\}\geq 4
.
It follows that
\beta=1-\alpha_{w}-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{w}=1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}
.
If
\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}\leq 1
, i.e.,
\alpha_{w}\leq\frac{2}{3}
,
then
\beta\geq 0
and thus
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\frac{\beta}{2}=\frac{1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}}{2}\leq\frac{1-\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{1}{3}}{2}=\frac{1}{4}
.

•

d(f_{uw})\geq 4
, and
d(f_{vw})\geq 4
.
It follows that
\beta=1-\alpha_{w}-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{w}-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{w}=1-2\alpha_{w}
.
If
2\alpha_{w}\leq 1
, i.e.,
\alpha_{w}\leq\frac{1}{2}
,
then
\beta\geq 0
and thus
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\frac{\beta}{2}=\frac{1-2\alpha_{w}}{2}\leq\frac{1-2\times\frac{1}{3}}{2}=\frac{1}{6}
.

Case 3.
n_{5}(f)=1
, i.e.,
d_{H}(v)\geq 6
and
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
.
In this case,
\tau(f\rightarrow v)_{f}=\alpha_{v}
,
\tau(f\rightarrow w)_{f}=\alpha_{w}
,
and
\tau(f\rightarrow w)_{f_{uv}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{v}
,

\tau(f\rightarrow w)_{f_{uw}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{w}
.
Recall that
\alpha_{v}\geq\frac{1}{3}
and
\alpha_{w}\geq\frac{1}{3}
.

•

d(f_{uw})=d(f_{vw})=3
.
It follows that
\beta=1-\alpha_{v}-\alpha_{w}
.
If
\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}\leq 1
, then
\beta\geq 0
and thus
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\beta=1-\alpha_{v}-\alpha_{w}\leq 1-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{1}{3}=\frac{1}{3}
.

•

d(f_{uv})\geq 4
,
d(f_{uw})=3
.
It follows that
\beta=1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}-\alpha_{w}
.
If
\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}\leq 1
,
then
\beta\geq 0
and thus
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\beta=1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}-\alpha_{w}\leq 1-\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{1}{3}-\frac{1}{3}=\frac{1}{6}
.

•

d(f_{uv})=3
,
d(f_{uw})\geq 4
.
It follows that
\beta=1-\alpha_{v}-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}
.
If
\alpha_{v}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}\leq 1
,
then
\beta\geq 0
and thus
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\beta=1-\alpha_{v}-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}\leq 1-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{1}{3}=\frac{1}{6}
.

•

d(f_{uv})\geq
,
d(f_{uw})\geq 4
.
It follows that
\beta=1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}
.
If
\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}\leq 1
, i.e.,
\alpha_{v}=\alpha_{w}=\frac{1}{3}
,
then
\beta\geq 0
and thus
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\beta=1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}=1-\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{1}{3}-\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{1}{3}=0
.

This finishes the proof of the lemma that
\tau(f\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{1}{3}
.

\Box

Discharging-Lemma
7
tells that the value of
\tau(f\rightarrow u)
depends on
n_{5}(f)
and some other factors.
One sees that for any
x
with
d_{H}(x)=k\geq 6
,
\alpha_{x}\geq 1-\frac{4}{k}
.
Hence, we have the following lemma to upper bound
\tau(f\rightarrow u)
.

Discharging-Lemma 8

For every
5
-vertex
u
in
V(H)
, let
f=[uvw]
be a
3
-face, and then we have the following:

(1)

If
d_{H}(v)=5
and
d_{H}(w)=k\geq 6
, then

\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\begin{cases}\frac{1-\alpha_{w}}{2}\leq\frac{2}{k},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uw})=d(f_{vw})=3,\mbox{ and }\alpha_{w}\leq 1;\\
\frac{1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}}{2}\leq\frac{3}{k}-\frac{1}{4},&\mbox{ if }3\in\{d(f_{uw}),d(f_{vw})\},\max\{d(f_{uw}),d(f_{vw})\}\geq 4,\mbox{ and }k\leq 12;\\
\frac{1-2\alpha_{w}}{2}\leq\frac{4}{k}-\frac{1}{2},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uw})\geq 4,d(f_{vw})\geq 4,\mbox{ and }k\leq 8.\end{cases}

(2)

If
d_{H}(v)=k_{1}\geq 6
and
d_{H}(w)=k_{2}\geq 6
, then

\tau(f\rightarrow u)=\begin{cases}1-\alpha_{v}-\alpha_{w}\leq\frac{4}{k_{1}}+\frac{4}{k_{2}}-1,&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uv})=d(f_{uw})=3,\mbox{ and }\frac{4}{k_{1}}+\frac{4}{k_{2}}-1\geq 0;\\
1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}-\alpha_{w}\leq\frac{6}{k_{1}}+\frac{4}{k_{2}}-\frac{3}{2},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uv})\geq 4,d(f_{uw})=3,\mbox{ and }\frac{6}{k_{1}}+\frac{4}{k_{2}}-\frac{3}{2}\geq 0;\\
1-\alpha_{v}-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}\leq\frac{4}{k_{1}}+\frac{6}{k_{2}}-\frac{3}{2},&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uv})=3,d(f_{uw})\geq 4,\mbox{ and }\frac{4}{k_{1}}+\frac{6}{k_{2}}-\frac{3}{2}\geq 0;\\
1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{v}-\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{w}\leq\frac{6}{k_{1}}+\frac{6}{k_{2}}-2=0,&\mbox{ if }d(f_{uv})\geq 4,d(f_{uw})\geq 4,\mbox{ and }k_{1}=k_{2}=6.\end{cases}

Figure 5:
Some special values for the upper bounds
\frac{2}{k}
,
\frac{3}{k}-\frac{1}{4}
, and
\frac{4}{k}-\frac{1}{2}
, where
k\geq 6
.

Figure 6:
Some special values for the upper bounds
\frac{4}{k_{1}}+\frac{4}{k_{2}}-1
and
\frac{6}{k_{1}}+\frac{4}{k_{2}}-1
, where
k_{1},k_{2}\geq 6
.

For convenience, for some small values of
k,k_{1},k_{2}\geq 6
,
we list the upper bounds stated in Discharging-Lemma
8
in the two tables depicted in Figure
5
and Figure
6
.
In the sequel, we will directly refer to these values.
Let
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4},u_{5}
denote all the neighbors of
u
in clockwise order,
and denote the face containing
u_{1}uu_{2}
(
u_{2}uu_{3}
,
u_{3}uu_{4}
,
u_{4}uu_{5}
,
u_{5}uu_{1}
, respectively) as
f_{1}
(
f_{2},f_{3},f_{4}
,
f_{5}
respectively).
Recall that for each
x\in N_{H}(u)
,
d_{H}(x)\geq 4
.

For a
3
-face
f=[vuw]
incident to
u
with
d_{H}(v)\geq 6
,
d_{H}(w)\geq 6
, and
\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}\geq 1
,
by (R5.2),
\tau(u\rightarrow f)=\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{w}-1\geq 0
.
Then
\tau(f\rightarrow u)=-\tau(u\rightarrow f)\leq 0
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}(u)=\omega(u)+\sum_{f\in F_{3}(v)}\tau(f\rightarrow u)=4-5+\sum_{f\in F_{3}(v)}\tau(f\rightarrow u)=-1+\sum_{f\in F_{3}(v)}\tau(f\rightarrow u).

(12)

We validate that
\omega^{\prime}(u)\leq 0
for each
5
-vertex
u
, using Eq. (
12
), in the following two lemmas.

Discharging-Lemma 9

For every
5
-vertex
u
adjacent with a
4
-vertex
u_{1}
in
V(H)
,
\omega^{\prime}(u)\leq 0
.

Proof.

One sees that for any
4
-vertex
x\in N_{H}(u)
,
d(x)=d_{H}(x)=4
and
the average weight transferred from each
3
-face in
F(ux)
to
u
is at most
\max\{\frac{1}{2}\times\frac{1}{10},\frac{1}{2}\times\frac{2}{5}\}=\frac{1}{5}
.
Thus, together with Discharging-Lemma
7
,
the average weight transferred from each
3
-face in
F_{3}(u)
to
u
is at most
\frac{1}{3}
.

When
m_{3}(u_{1})\neq 4
, or
m_{3}(u_{1})=4
and
\min\{d(f_{2}),d(f_{4})\}\geq 4
, no weight is transferred from any face in
F(uu_{1})
to
u
;
since the average weight transferred from each
3
-face in
\{f_{2},f_{3},f_{4}\}
is at most
\frac{1}{3}
, Eq. (
12
) becomes

\omega^{\prime}(u)=-1+\sum_{f\in\{f_{2},f_{3},f_{4}\}}\tau(f\rightarrow u)\leq-1+3\times\frac{1}{3}=0
.

When
m_{3}(u_{1})=4
and
\min\{d(f_{2}),d(f_{4})\}=3
, it follows from Lemma
2
(2.4) that for each
x\in\{u_{2},u_{5}\}
,
d_{H}(x)\geq 8
and
d(x)\geq 10
;
thus, by Lemma
4
(1) and (4),
\alpha_{x}\geq\frac{3}{5}
.
Using (R3), (R4), (R5) and Discharging-Lemma
7
, we discuss the following three subcases on
d(f_{i})
,
i\in\{2,3,4\}
.

(1)

\max\{d(f_{2}),d(f_{4})\}\geq 4
, and assuming w.l.o.g.
d(f_{2})=3
and
d(f_{4})\geq 4
.
In this case,
\tau(F(uu_{1})\rightarrow u)=\frac{1}{10}
.
Since the average weight transferred from each
3
-face in
\{f_{2},f_{3}\}
is at most
\frac{1}{3}
, Eq. (
12
) becomes

\omega^{\prime}(u)=-1+\sum_{f\in\{f_{1},f_{5}\}}\tau(f\rightarrow u)\leq-1+\frac{1}{10}+2\times\frac{1}{3}=-\frac{7}{30}<0
.

(2)

d(f_{2})=d(f_{4})=3
and
d(f_{3})\geq 4
.
In this case,
\tau(F(uu_{1})\rightarrow u)=\frac{2}{5}
.
One sees that if
d_{H}(u_{3})=4
, no weight will be transferred from
f_{2}
to
u
;
if
d_{H}(u_{3})=5
, then
\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{1-\alpha_{u_{2}}}{2}\leq\frac{1-\frac{3}{5}}{2}=\frac{1}{5}
;
if
d_{H}(u_{3})\geq 6
, since
\frac{3}{2}\times\alpha_{u_{3}}+\alpha_{u_{2}}\geq\frac{3}{2}\times\frac{1}{3}+\alpha_{u_{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3}{5}=\frac{11}{10}
,
then we have
\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u)\leq 0
.
It follows that
\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{1}{5}
; and likewise,
\tau(f_{4}\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{1}{5}
.
Hence, Eq. (
12
) becomes

\omega^{\prime}(u)=-1+\sum_{f\in\{f_{1},f_{5}\}}\tau(f\rightarrow u)+\sum_{f\in\{f_{2},f_{4}\}}\tau(f\rightarrow u)\leq-1+\frac{2}{5}+2\times\frac{1}{5}=-\frac{1}{5}<0
.

(3)

d(f_{3})=d(f_{2})=d(f_{4})=3
.
In this case,
\tau(F(uu_{1})\rightarrow u)=\frac{2}{5}
.

–

\min\{d_{H}(u_{3}),d_{H}(u_{4})\}=4
, and assuming w.l.o.g.
d(u_{3})=d_{H}(u_{3})=4
.
It follows that
d_{H}(u_{4})\geq 8
and
d(u_{4})\geq 10
,
and then
\tau(F(uu_{3})\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{2}{5}
and
\tau(u\rightarrow f_{4})=\alpha_{u_{5}}+\alpha_{u_{4}}-1\geq 2\times\frac{3}{5}-1=\frac{1}{5}
,
i.e.,
\tau(f_{4}\rightarrow u)\leq-\frac{1}{5}
.
Hence, Eq. (
12
) becomes

\omega^{\prime}(u)=-1+\sum_{f\in\{f_{1},f_{5}\}}\tau(f\rightarrow u)+\sum_{f\in\{f_{2},f_{3}\}}\tau(f\rightarrow u)+\tau(f_{4}\rightarrow u)\leq-1+2\times\frac{2}{5}-\frac{1}{5}=-\frac{2}{5}<0
.

–

\min\{d_{H}(u_{3}),d_{H}(u_{4})\}=5
, and assuming w.l.o.g.
d(u_{3})=d_{H}(u_{3})=5
.
The non-existence of (
A_{7.1}
) in
G
states that
d_{H}(u_{4})\geq 7
.
Then we have
\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{1-\alpha_{u_{2}}}{2}\leq\frac{1-\frac{3}{5}}{2}=\frac{1}{5}
,

\tau(f_{3}\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{1-\alpha_{u_{4}}}{2}\leq\frac{1-\frac{3}{7}}{2}=\frac{2}{7}
,
and since
\alpha_{u_{5}}+\alpha_{u_{4}}\geq\frac{3}{5}+\frac{3}{7}=\frac{36}{35}
,

\tau(u\rightarrow f_{4})=\alpha_{u_{5}}+\alpha_{u_{4}}-1\geq\frac{36}{35}-1=\frac{1}{35}
, i.e.,
\tau(f_{4}\rightarrow u)\leq-\frac{1}{35}
.
Hence, Eq. (
12
) becomes

\omega^{\prime}(u)=-1+\sum_{f\in\{f_{1},f_{5}\}}\tau(f\rightarrow u)+\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u)+\tau(f_{3}\rightarrow u)+\tau(f_{4}\rightarrow u)\leq-1+\frac{2}{5}+\frac{1}{5}+\frac{2}{7}-\frac{1}{35}=-\frac{1}{7}<0
.

–

d_{H}(u_{3})\geq 6
and
d_{H}(u_{4})\geq 6
.
Then we have
\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u)=1-\alpha_{u_{2}}-\alpha_{u_{3}}\leq 1-\frac{3}{5}-\frac{1}{3}=\frac{1}{15}
,

\tau(f_{3}\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{1}{3}
, and
\tau(f_{4}\rightarrow u)=1-\alpha_{u_{5}}-\alpha_{u_{4}}\leq 1-\frac{3}{5}-\frac{1}{3}=\frac{1}{15}
.
Hence, Eq. (
12
) becomes

\omega^{\prime}(u)=-1+\sum_{f\in\{f_{1},f_{5}\}}\tau(f\rightarrow u)+\tau(f_{2}\rightarrow u)+\tau(f_{3}\rightarrow u)+\tau(f_{4}\rightarrow u)\leq-1+\frac{2}{5}+\frac{1}{15}+\frac{1}{3}+\frac{1}{15}=-\frac{2}{15}<0
.

This proves the lemma.

\Box

Discharging-Lemma 10

For every
5
-vertex
u
in
V(H)
, if
d_{H}(x)\geq 5
for each
x\in N_{H}(u)
, then
\omega^{\prime}(u)\leq 0
.

Proof.

It follows from Discharging-Lemma
7
that for each face
f\in F_{3}(u)
,
\tau(f\rightarrow u)\leq\frac{1}{3}
.
When
m_{3}(f)\leq 3
, Eq. (
12
) becomes
\omega^{\prime}(u)\leq-1+3\times\frac{1}{3}=0
.

When there exist two faces
f=[\ldots v_{1}uw_{1}\ldots\}
and
h=[\ldots v_{2}uw_{2}]
such that for any
x\in\{v_{1},w_{1}\}\cup\{v_{2},w_{2}\}
,
\alpha_{x}\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
one sees that if
d(f)
= 3, then
\tau(u\rightarrow f)=\alpha_{v_{1}}+\alpha_{w_{1}}-1\geq 2\times\frac{1}{2}-1\geq 0
, i.e.,
\tau(f\rightarrow u)\leq 0
.
It follows that
\tau(f\rightarrow u)\leq 0
, and likewise
\tau(g\rightarrow u)\leq 0
, and Eq. (
12
) becomes

\omega^{\prime}(u)=-1+\sum_{f\in F_{3}(v)\setminus\{f,h\}}\tau(f\rightarrow u)\leq-1+3\times\frac{1}{3}=0
.

If there exist
v,w\in N_{H}(u)
such that
d_{H}(v)=5
and
d_{H}(w)\leq 6
, i.e.,
d(v)=5
and
d(w)\leq 6
,

f=[vuw]
is a
3
-face and
m_{3}(uv)=2
,
then the non-existence of (
2.1
), (
6.3
) and (
7.1
) in
G
imply that for each
x\in N_{H}(u)\setminus\{v,w\}
,
d(x)\geq 8
,
and since
d(u)=5
, we have
d_{H}(x)\geq 8
and thus
\alpha_{x}\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

Recall that if
d_{H}(x)\geq 8
or
x
is
(7,5^{+})_{H}
, then
\alpha_{x}\geq\frac{1}{2}
;
if
d_{H}(x)\leq 6
, then
d(x)=d_{H}(x)\leq 6
;
and if
x
is
(7,4^{-})_{H}
, then
x
is
(7,4^{-})_{HG}
.
In the other case,
4\leq m_{3}(f)\leq 5
.
In the sequel, we continue the proof with the following propositions which summarize the above argument:

(I)

For each
v,w\in N_{H}(x)
with
vw\in E(H)
and
m_{3}(uv)=2
, if
d_{H}(v)=5
, i.e.,
d(v)=5
, then
d_{H}(w)\geq 7
and
\alpha_{w}\geq\frac{3}{7}
; if
d_{H}(w)\leq 6
, i.e.,
d(w)\leq 6
, then
d_{H}(v)\geq 6
and
\alpha_{v}\geq\frac{1}{3}
;

(II)

If
\alpha_{x}<\frac{1}{2}
for some

, then
d(x)=d_{H}(x)\leq 6
or
x
is
(7,4^{-})_{HG}
.

Assume w.l.o.g.
d(f_{i})=3
, for any

, and
d(f_{5})\geq 3
.
Using (R3.1), (R3.2), (R5.1), and (R5.2), we discuss the following two cases for two possible values of
m_{3}(u)
.

.

(13)

Otherwise,
\alpha_{u_{5}}<\frac{5}{9}
.
Hence,

, or

is
(7,5^{-})_{HG}
, or
u_{5}
is
(8,4^{-})_{HG}
.

•

.

.

.

.

3
Acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring

In this section, we show how to derive an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for a simple
2
-connected planar graph
G
,
by an induction on the number of edges
|E(G)|
.
The following lemma gives the starting point.

Lemma 5

(
[
16
,
3
,
4
,
15
,
19
]
)

If
\Delta\in\{3,4\}
, then
a^{\prime}(G)\leq\Delta+2
, and an acyclic edge
(\Delta+2)
-coloring can be obtained in polynomial time.
2
2

2

[
16
]
gives an
O(n)
-time algorithm for
\Delta=3
;

[
4
]
gives a polynomial-time algorithm for
\Delta=4
except
4
-regular;

[
15
,
19
]
gives a polynomial-time algorithm for
4
-regular graphs.

Given a partial acyclic edge
k
-coloring
c(\cdot)
of the graph
G
using the color set
C=\{1,2,\ldots,k\}
,
for a vertex
v\in V(G)
, let
C(v)
denote the set of colors assigned to the edges incident at
v
under
c(\cdot)
.
If the edges of a path
P=ux\ldots v
are alternatively colored
i
and
j
, we call it an
(i,j)_{(u,v)}
-path
.
Furthermore, if
uv\in E(G)
is also colored
i
or
j
, we call
ux\ldots vu
an
(i,j)_{(u,v)}
-cycle

(which implies
c(\cdot)
is invalid and needs to be revised).

For simplicity, we use
\{e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{m}\}\to a
to state that all the edges
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
,
\ldots
,
e_{m}
are colored
a
,
use simply
e_{1}\to a
to state that
e_{1}
is colored
a
,
use
e_{1}\to S
(
S\neq\emptyset
) to state that
e_{1}
is colored with a color in
S
,
and use
(e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{m})\to(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{m})
to state that
e_{j}
is colored
a_{j}
, for
j=1,2,\ldots,m
.
We also use
(e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{m})_{c}=(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{m})
to denote that
c(e_{j})=a_{j}
, for
j=1,2,\ldots,m
;
that is, to emphasize the coloring is by
c(\cdot)
.
Moreover, we use
i_{1}/i_{2}/\ldots/i_{k}\in S
to state that at least one of
i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k}
is in
S
, i.e.,
S\cap\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k}\}\neq\emptyset
.
By a graph
H
containing an
(S_{1},S_{2})_{(u,v)}
-path
(an
(a,S)_{(u,v)}
-path, an
(a,i_{1}/i_{2}/\ldots/i_{k})_{(u,v)}
-path, respectively),
it means that
H
contains an
(i,j)_{(u,v)}
-path for each pair
i\in S_{1}
,
j\in S_{2}

(an
(a,j)_{(u,v)}
-path for each
j\in S
, an
(a,j)_{(u,v)}
-path for some
j\in\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k}\}
, respectively).

The following lemma is obvious (via a simple contradiction):

Lemma 6

[
15
]

Suppose
G
has an acyclic edge coloring
c(\cdot)
,
and
P=uv_{1}v_{2}
-
\ldots
-
v_{k}v_{k+1}
is a maximal
(a,b)_{(u,v_{k+1})}
-path with
c(uv_{1})=a
and
b\not\in C(u)
.
Then there is no
(a,b)_{(u,w)}
-path for any vertex
w\not\in V(P)
.

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
1
, by an induction on the number of edges
|E(G)|
.
The flow of the proof is depicted in Figure
7
.

Figure 7:
The flow of the proof of Theorem
1
by an induction on
|E(G)|
.

For the base cases in the induction,
one sees that when
|E(G)|\leq\Delta+5
, coloring each edge by a distinct color gives a valid acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring;
when
\Delta\leq 4
, an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring is guaranteed by Lemma
5
.

3.0 Configuration (
A_{1}
), the simplest cases of the inductive step

In the sequel we consider a simple
2
-connected planar graph
G
such that
\Delta\geq 5
and
|E(G)|\geq\Delta+6
.
We first use some simplest cases to convince the readers that the inductive step works,
then starting Subsection 3.1 the inductive step becomes more complex yet using the similar ideas.
Theorem
2
states that
G
contains at least one of the configurations (
A_{1}
)–(
A_{8}
),
which are the cases to be discussed inside the inductive step.

As the most simplest case of the inductive step, consider the configuration (
A_{1.1}
) in
G
.
Let
G^{\prime}=G-v+uw
, that is, by merging the two edges
uv
and
vw
into a new edge
uw
while eliminating the vertex
v
.
The graph
G^{\prime}
is planar,
\Delta(G^{\prime})=\Delta
, and contains one less edge than
G
.
Using the inductive hypothesis,
G^{\prime}
has an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring
c(\cdot)
using the color set
C=\{1,2,\ldots,\Delta+5\}
,
in which we assume that
c(uw)=1
.
To construct an edge coloring for
G
,
we keep the color for every edge of
E(G^{\prime})\setminus\{uw\}
,
vw\to 1
, and
uv\to C\setminus C(u)
.
One may trivially verify that no bichromatic cycle would be introduced, that is, the achieved edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
is acyclic.

One sees that for the edge
uv
in each of the other configurations,
we have the degree
d(v)\in\{2,3\}
(in (
A_{1.2}
), (
A_{1.3}
), (
A_{2}
)–(
A_{5}
) respectively),
or the degree
d(u)\in\{4,5\}
(in (
A_{6}
)–(
A_{8}
) respectively).
We pick the edge
uv
and let
H=G-uv
(that is,
H
is obtained by removing the edge
uv
from
G
, or precisely
H=(V(G),E(G)\setminus\{uv\})
).
The graph
H
is planar, and
4\leq\Delta-1\leq\Delta(H)\leq\Delta
.
3
3

3

Note that
H
might not be
2
-connected, but it does not matter.

Using the
inductive hypothesis
,
H
has an acyclic edge
(\Delta(H)+5)
-coloring
c(\cdot)
,
and thus a
(\Delta+5)
-coloring, using the color set
C=\{1,2,\ldots,\Delta+5\}
.

We have
d_{H}(u)+d_{H}(v)\leq\Delta-1+5-1=\Delta+3<|C|
, or equivalently
C\setminus(C(u)\cup C(v))\neq\emptyset
.
One sees that,
if
C(u)\cap C(v)=\emptyset
, then
uv\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}C\setminus(C(u)\cup C(v))
.
We define the notation
\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}
to mean that
uv\to C\setminus(C(u)\cup C(v))
gives an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
and thus we are done.
Or, if there exists a
j\in C\setminus(C(u)\cup C(v))
such that, for all
i\in C(u)\cap C(v)
,
H
contains no
(i,j)_{(u,v)}
-path,
then
uv\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}j
.
In the remaining case, for each
i\in C(u)\cap C(v)
, we assume that
c(uu_{s})=c(vv_{t})=i
,
and we define the color set

B_{i}=\{j\mid\mbox{there is an }(i,j)_{(u_{s},v_{t})}\mbox{-path in }H\}.

Therefore, for each
j\in B_{i}
, there is a neighbor
x
of
u_{s}
and a neighbor
y
of
v_{t}
, respectively,
such that
c(u_{s}x)=c(v_{t}y)=j
, and each of
x
and
y
is incident with an edge colored
i
.
One sees that
B_{i}\subseteq C(u_{s})\cap C(v_{t})
.
In the sequel we continue the proof with the following Proposition
3.0.0.1
4
4

4

Throughout the paper, we characterize a lot of intermediate properties, but not final conclusions,
that are useful for down-stream proofs into
propositions
.
These propositions are subsection numbered in four levels.
When citing them, we only use the 4-level labels, without mentioning “Proposition”.
,
or otherwise we are done:

Proposition 3.0.0.1

C(u)\cap C(v)\neq\emptyset
, and
C\setminus(C(u)\cup C(v))\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in C(u)\cap C(v)}B_{i}
.

For each degree
k\in\{2,3,4,5\}
,
we collect the colors of the edges connecting
u
and its
k
-neighbors (
k^{-}
-neighbors, respectively) into

S_{k}(u)=\{c(uw)\mid w\in N(u)\setminus\{v\},d(w)=k\}
(
S_{k^{-}}(u)=\{c(uw)\mid w\in N(u)\setminus\{v\},d(w)\leq k\}
, respectively).

The second simplest cases would be the configurations (
A_{1.2}
) and (
A_{1.3}
), in which there is a path
uvw
with
d(v)=2
.
Let
u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{d(u)-2}
be the other neighbors of
u
.
Using the acyclic edge coloring
c(\cdot)
for
H=G-uv
,
by
3.0.0.1
,
c(vw)\in C(u)
,
H
contains a
(c(vw),i)_{(u,w)}
-path for every

,
and thus
(C\setminus C(u))\subseteq C(w)\setminus\{c(vw),c(uw)\}
.
Let
c(uu_{i})=i
for each
1\leq i\leq d(u)-2
and
(vw,uw)_{c}=(1,d(u)-1)
.
It follows that (
A_{1.3}
) holds and
[d(u),\Delta+5]\subseteq C(w)
.
One sees that
|[d(u),\Delta+5]|\geq\Delta+5-(d(u)-1)=\Delta-d(u)+6\geq 6
.
Since
n_{5^{-}}(u)\geq d(u)-6
,
S_{5^{-}}(u)\geq d(u)-7
and
|S_{5^{-}}(u)\cup(C\setminus C(u))\cup\{1,d(u)-1\}|\geq d(u)-7+(\Delta-d(u)+6)+2=\Delta+1
.
It follows that
6\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(w)
.
Then
6\to vw
and

give rise to an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
, and we are done.

We have thus far demonstrated that the inductive step works for the case where

contains the configuration (
A_{1}
),
i.e., one of the configurations (
A_{1.1}
)–(

),
and summarize in the following lemma, where the running time refers to the time spent during the inductive step.

Lemma 7

If (
A_{1}
) holds, then in
O(1)
time an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
can be obtained.

The next two subsections deal with the configurations (

)–(

) and (

)–(

In Subsection 3.1, we first deal with (

,
and then
|C(u)\cap C(v)|=2
in Lemma

,
which says that in

or an acyclic edge

-coloring for

can be obtained such that

;
next, we deal with (

) in Subsection 3.1.1, (

) in Subsection 3.1.2, and (

In Subsection 3.2, we first consider the condition where

in Lemma

,

|C(u)\cap C(v)|=3
in Lemma

,
and
|C(u)\cap C(v)|=1
in Lemma

,
which says that in

time either an acyclic edge

can be obtained,
or an acyclic edge

-coloring for

next, we deal with

in Subsection 3.2.1,

In particular, in Subsection 3.2.2., we distinguish two cases for the two possible values

.
It takes a long time in Subsection 3.2.2 to verify that if

),

We remark that this process is the most interesting.

3.1
Configurations (
A_{2}
)–(
A_{5}
)

In this subsection we prove the inductive step for the case where
G
contains one of the configurations (
A_{2}
)–(
A_{5}
).
We have the following revised (
A_{2}
)–(
A_{5}
):
An edge
uv
with
d(v)=3
and
d(u)=k\leq 11
such that at least one of the configurations (
A_{2}
)–(
A_{5}
) occurs.

Lemma 8

If (
A_{2.2}
) holds, then in
O(1)
time an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
can be obtained.

Proof.

Let
G^{\prime}=G-v+v_{1}v_{2}
, that is, merging the two edges
v_{1}v
and
vv_{2}
into a new edge
v_{1}v_{2}
while eliminating the vertex
v
.
The graph
G^{\prime}
is a planar graph,
\Delta(G^{\prime})=\Delta
, and contains two less edges than
G
.
By the inductive hypothesis,
G^{\prime}
has an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring
c(\cdot)
using the color set
C=\{1,2,\ldots,\Delta+5\}
,
in which
C(x)
is the color set of the edges incident at
x\in\{u,v_{1},v_{2}\}
,
and we assume that
(uv_{1},uv_{2})_{c}=(2,3)
and
c(uu_{i})=i
,
i\in\{1,4,5,\ldots,k-1\}
,
where
u_{1},u_{4},\ldots,u_{k-1}
are the neighbors of
u
other that
v,v_{1},v_{2}
.

To transform back an edge coloring for
G
,
we keep the color for every edge of
E(G^{\prime})\setminus\{v_{1}v_{2}\}
, and color
\{uv,vv_{1},vv_{2}\}
as follows by recoloring some edges incident to
u
if needed.

Case 1.

c(v_{1}v_{2})\not\in C(u)
, assuming w.l.o.g.
c(v_{1}v_{2})=k
.

When
[k+1,\Delta+5]\setminus C(v_{2})\neq\emptyset
,
vv_{1}\to k
,

vv_{2}\to\alpha\in[k+1,\Delta+5]\setminus C(v_{2})
,
and
uv\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}[k+1,\Delta+5]\setminus\{\alpha\}
.

In the other case,
[k+1,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(v_{2})
and likewise,
[k+1,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(v_{1})
.
Since
u
is
([8,11],5^{-})
,
|S_{5^{-}}(u)|\geq k-6
.
One sees that
|S_{5^{-}}(u)\cup[k+1,\Delta+5]|\geq k-6+\Delta+5-k=\Delta-k-1
.
It follows that there exists an
i_{0}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{1})
.
Then
(vv_{1},vv_{2})\to(i_{0},k)
and
uv\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}[k+1,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{0}})
.

Case 2.

c(v_{1}v_{2})\in C(u)
, assuming w.l.o.g.
c(v_{1}v_{2})=1
.

When
[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(v_{i})\neq\emptyset
for some
i\in\{1,2\}
, assuming w.l.o.g.
k\not\in C(v_{2})
,
if there is no
(1,j_{0})_{(u_{1},v_{1})}
-path for some
j_{0}\in[k+1,\Delta+5]
,
then
(vv_{1},vv_{2},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(1,k,j_{0})
;
otherwise,
G
contains a
(1,j)_{(u_{1},v_{1})}
-path for each
j\in[k+1,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(v_{1})
.
Since
|[k+1,\Delta+5]|=\Delta-k+5\geq 5
and
S_{5^{-}}(u)\geq k-6
,
there exists an
j_{1}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{1})
.
Then
(vv_{1},vv_{2})\to(j_{1},1)
and
uv\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}[k+1,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{j_{1}})
.

In the other case,
[k,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(v_{1})\cap C(v_{2})
.
Note that
|[k,\Delta+5]|=\Delta-k+6\geq 6
.
When there exists
j_{2}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus(C(v_{1})\cup C(v_{2}))
,
let
\beta\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{j_{2}})
,
by Lemma
6
,
(vv_{1},vv_{2},uv)\to(1,j_{2},\beta)
or
(vv_{1},vv_{2},uv)\to(j_{2},1,\beta)
gives rise to an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
.

Now we assume that
S_{5^{-}}(u)\subseteq(C(v_{1})\cup C(v_{2}))
.
Since
2+|[k,\Delta+5]|+2+|[k,\Delta+5]|+|S_{5^{-}}(u)|=2\Delta+10-k\leq d(v_{1})+d(v_{2})\leq 2\Delta
, we have
k\geq 10
.
Since
2+|[k,\Delta+5]|+|S_{5^{-}}(u)|=2+\Delta+6-k+k-6=\Delta+2
,
we have
|S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{1})|\geq 2
and
|S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{2})|\geq 2
.

For each
i_{1}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{1})
and
i\in C\setminus C(v_{2})
,
if
G
contains no
(1,j)_{(v_{2},u_{1})}
-path for some
j\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{1}})
,
then
(vv_{1},vv_{2},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(i_{1},1,j)
;
otherwise,
G
contains a
(1,j)_{(v_{2},u_{1})}
-path for each
j\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{1}})
;
if
G
contains no
(i,j)_{(v_{2},u)}
-path for some
j\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{1}})
,
then
(vv_{1},vv_{2},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(1,i,j)
.
In the other case, we proceed with the following proposition:

Proposition 3.1.0.1

(1)

for each
i_{1}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{1})
and
i\in C\setminus C(v_{2})
,

G
contains a
(1,j)_{(v_{2},u_{1})}
-path and a
(i,j)_{(v_{2},u_{i_{2}})}
-path for each
j\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{1}})
;

(2)

for each
i_{2}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{2})
and
i\in C\setminus C(v_{1})
,

G
contains a
(1,j)_{(v_{1},u_{1})}
-path and a
(i,j)_{(v_{1},u_{i_{1}})}
-path for each
j\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{2}})
;

(3)

for each
i_{1}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{1})
and for each
i_{2}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{2})
,

[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{1}})\subseteq C(u_{i_{2}})
,

[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{2}})\subseteq C(u_{i_{1}})
,
and
[k,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(u_{i_{1}})\cup C(u_{i_{2}})
.

For each
i_{2}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{2})
,
when for some
j\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{2}})
,
G
contains no
(i,j)_{(u_{i_{2}},u)}
-path for any
i\in C(u)
,
it follows from
3.1.0.1
that
G
contains a
(1,j)_{(v_{1},u_{1})}
-path.
If
G
contains no
(1,l)_{(u_{1},v_{1})}
-path for some
[k,\Delta+5]\setminus\{j\}
,
then
(vv_{1},vv_{2},uu_{i_{2}},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(1,i_{2},j,l)
.
Otherwise,
G
contains a
(1,l)_{(u_{1},v_{1})}
-path for any
l\in[k,\Delta+5]
.
Then
(vv_{1},vv_{2},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(i_{1},1,l)
.
In the other case, we proceed with the following proposition:

Proposition 3.1.0.2

(1)

For each
j\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{2}})
, where
i_{2}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{2})
,
G
contains a
(i,j)_{(u_{i_{2}},u)}
-path for some
i\in C(u)
.

(2)

For each
j\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{i_{1}})
, where
i_{1}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{1})
,
G
contains a
(i,j)_{(u_{i_{1}},u)}
-path for some
i\in C(u)
.

It follows that
|C(u_{i_{1}})\cap[k,\Delta+5]|\leq 3
and
|C(u_{i_{2}})\cap[k,\Delta+5]|\leq 3
.
Since
|[k,\Delta+5]|\geq 6
, we have
|C(u_{i_{1}})\cap[k,\Delta+5]|=3
,
|C(u_{i_{2}})\cap[k,\Delta+5]|=3
, and
\Delta=k
.
Further, by Propositions
3.1.0.1
and
3.1.0.2
,
we assume that for each
i_{2}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{2})
,
C(u_{i_{2}})=\{a_{i_{2}},k,k+1,k+2\}
;
and for each
i_{1}\in S_{5^{-}}(u)\setminus C(v_{1})
,
C(u_{i_{1}})=\{a_{i_{1}},k+3,k+4,k+5\}
.

We consider below the case where
k=10
and
k=11
, respectively.

Case 2.1.
k=10
, and
C(v_{1})=\{2,6,7\}\cup[10,16]
,

C(v_{2})=\{3,8,9\}\cup[10,16]
.
Then
C(u_{6})=\{a_{6},10,11,12\}
,
C(u_{7})=\{a_{7},10,11,12\}
,
and by Propositions
3.1.0.1
,
G
contains a
(i,15)_{(u,v_{1})}
-path for any
i\in\{1,3,4,5,8,9\}
.
However, by Propositions
3.1.0.2
,
G
contains a
(2,15)_{(v_{1},u_{6})}
-path and a
(2,15)_{(v_{1},u_{7})}
-path, a contradiction.

Case 2.2.
k=11
.
Assume w.l.o.g.
C(v_{1})=\{2,6,7,10\}\cup[11,\Delta+5]
,

\{3,8,9\}\cup[11,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(v_{2})
.
Then
C(u_{6})=\{a_{6},11,12,13\}
,
C(u_{7})=\{a_{7},11,12,13\}
,
and
C(u_{8})=\{a_{8},14,15,16\}
,
C(u_{9})=\{a_{9},14,15,16\}
,
where
a_{i}\in C(u)
for
i\in[6,9]
.
It follows from Propositions
3.1.0.1
that
G
contains a
(i,15)_{(u,v_{1})}
-path for any
i\in\{1,3,4,5,8,9\}
and a
(1,11)_{(u_{1},v_{2})}
-path.

Hence, by Propositions
3.1.0.2
,
G
contains a
(i,15)_{(u,u_{6})}
-path and a
(j,15)_{(u,u_{7})}
-path for some
i,j\in\{2,10\}
.
By Lemma
3
, we assume w.l.o.g.
G
contains a
(2,15)_{(v_{1},u_{6})}
-path and a
(10,15)_{(u_{10},u_{7})}
-path,
and
C(u_{6})=\{2,6,11,12,13\}
,
C(u_{7})=\{7,10,11,12,13\}
.
It follows from Propositions
3.1.0.2
that
G
contains a
(10,j)_{(u_{10},u_{7})}
-path for each
j\in\{14,15,16\}
, and assume w.l.o.g.
11\not\in C(u_{10})
.

If
10\not\in C(v_{2})
, then
(vv_{1},vv_{2},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(1,10,11)
.
Otherwise,
C(v_{2})=\{3\}\cup[8,16]
.
It follows from Propositions
3.1.0.1
that
G
contains a
(i,11)_{(v_{2},u)}
-path for each
i\in\{1,2,4,5,6,7\}
.
However, by Propositions
3.1.0.2
,
G
contains a
(3,11)_{(v_{2},u_{8})}
-path and a
(3,11)_{(v_{2},u_{8})}
-path, a contradiction.

\Box

By Lemma
8
, we consider below the case where
G
contains the configuration (
A_{2}
)–(
A_{5}
) other that (
A_{2.2}
).
Let
u_{1}
,
u_{2}
,
\ldots
,
u_{k-1}
be the other neighbors of
u
other than
v
,
and assume that
c(uu_{i})=i
for
i=1,2,\ldots,k-1
, i.e.,
C(u)=[1,k-1]
;
let
v_{1}
,
v_{2}
be the neighbors of
v
other than
u
.

Before we discuss the detailed configurations,
we do not distinguish
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
but use
x_{1}
to refer to any one of
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
while
x_{2}
to refer to the other (
\{x_{1},x_{2}\}=\{u_{1},u_{2}\}
).
In particular,
u_{1}=x_{1}
in (
A_{2.3}
), (
A_{2.4}
), and (
A_{3}
)–(
A_{5}
), and
u_{2}=x_{2}
in (
A_{2.4}
), and (
A_{3}
)–(
A_{5}
).
It follows from
3.0.0.1
that
1\leq|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq 2
,
and
C\setminus(C(u)\cup C(v))\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in C(u)\cap C(v)}B_{i}
.

Lemma 9

If
|C(u)\cap C(v)|=2
, then in
O(1)
time either an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
can be obtained,
or an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
can be obtained such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|=1
.

Proof.

Assume w.l.o.g. that
(vx_{1},vx_{2})_{c}=(a,b)
, where
a,b\in[1,k-1]
.
When there exists a color
j\in[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(x_{i})
for some
i\in\{1,2\}
,

vx_{i}\to j
gives rise to an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|=1
.
In the other case, we proceed with the following proposition:

Proposition 3.1.0.3

(1)

[k,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(u_{a})\cup C(u_{b})
;
(2)

[k,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(x_{1})\cap C(x_{2})
.

Case 1.
(
A_{2.1}
) holds, i.e.,
d(u)=7
, and
C(u)=[1,6]
.

Assuming w.l.o.g.
\{a,b\}=\{1,2\}
.
It follows from
3.1.0.3
that
C(x_{1})=\{1\}\cup[7,\Delta+5]
and
C(x_{2})=\{2\}\cup[7,\Delta+5]
.
Since
7\in B_{1}\cup B_{2}
, assume w.l.o.g.
7\in B_{1}
.

If
G
contains no
(i,7)_{(x_{1},u_{i})}
-path for some
i\in\{2,3\}
,
then
(vx_{1},vx_{2},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(i,1,7)
.
Otherwise, for each
i\in\{2,3\}
,
G
contains a
(i,7)_{(x_{1},u_{i})}
-path and
7\in C(u_{i})
.
Likewise, we have
[7,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(u_{1})\cap C(u_{2})\cap C(u_{3})
and thus
C(u_{i})=\{i\}\cup[7,\Delta+5]
for every
i\in\{1,2,3\}
.
By Lemma
6
, there is neither a
(2,7)_{(u_{2},x_{2})}
-path nor a
(3,7)_{(u_{1},x_{1})}
-path,
and
(uu_{1},uu_{3},vx_{1},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(3,1,3,7)
.

Case 2.
(
A_{2.3}
) holds, i.e.,
u
is
(8,4^{-})
,
v_{2}
is
([8,11],5^{-})
, and
uv_{1},v_{1}v_{2}\in E(G)
,
uv_{2}\not\in E(G)
.

Assuming w.l.o.g.
c(vx_{1})=2
and
c(vx_{2})\in C(u)\setminus\{2\}
.
It follows from
3.1.0.3
that
C(x_{1})=\{1,2\}\cup[8,\Delta+5]
with
c(x_{1}x_{2})=8
,
and
\{c(vx_{2})\}\cup[8,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(x_{2})
.

When
c(vx_{2})\in C(u)\setminus\{1,2\}
, assuming w.l.o.g.
c(vx_{2})=3
,
since
8\in B_{2}\cup B_{3}
and
3\not\in C(x_{1})
, we have
2\in C(x_{2})
and
C(x_{2})=\{2,3\}\cup[8,\Delta+5]
;
then
(vx_{1},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(4,8)
.

In the other case,
c(vx_{2})=1
.
One sees from
|[3,7]\cap C(x_{2})|\leq 1
that there exists an
i\in[3,7]\setminus C(x_{2})
.
Then
vx_{2}\to i
reduces to the case where
c(vx_{2})\in C(u)\setminus\{1,2\}
.

Case 3.
(
A_{2.4}
) holds, i.e.,
d(u)=8
, and
uv_{1},uv_{2},v_{1}v_{2}\in E(G)
,
C(u)=[1,7]
.

It follows from
3.1.0.1
that
C(x_{1})=\{1,a\}\cup[8,\Delta+5]
and
C(x_{2})=\{2,b\}\cup[8,\Delta+5]
with
c(x_{1}x_{2})=8
.
Since
8\in B_{a}\cup B_{b}
, we assume w.l.o.g.
a=2
and
b=3
.
Then
(vx_{1},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(4,8)
.

Case 4.
(
A_{3}
)–(
A_{5}
) holds, i.e.,
u
is
([9,11],5^{-})
, and
uv_{1},uv_{2},v_{1}v_{2}\in E(G)
.

Assuming w.l.o.g.
u_{i}
be a
6^{+}
-vertex,
i\in[3,5]
, and
u_{i}
be a
5^{-}
-vertex,
i\in[6,k-1]
.
It follows from
3.1.0.1
that

[k,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(x_{1})
and
[k,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(x_{2})
.

For any
i\in\{a,b\}\cap S_{5^{-}}(u)
, say
6\in\{a,b\}
,
when
C(u_{6})\setminus\{6\}\subseteq[k,\Delta+5]
,
uu_{6}\to[k,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{6})
and an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|=1
is obtained.
In the other case, we proceed with the following proposition:

Proposition 3.1.0.4

For any
i\in\{a,b\}\cap S_{5^{-}}(u)
,
|C(u_{i})\setminus[k,\Delta+5]|\geq 2
and
|C(u_{i})\cap[k,\Delta+5]|\leq 3
.

Since
|\{a,b\}\cap\{1,2\}|\leq 1
,
|[3,k-1]\cap\{a,b\}|\geq 1
and we discuss the following three
subcases for three possible values of
\{a,b\}
, respectively.

Case 4.1.
a,b\in S_{5^{-}}(u)
, assuming w.l.o.g.
a=6
,
b=7
.
Since
|[k,\Delta+5]|\geq 6
,
it follows from
3.1.0.4
that
\Delta=k
and assume
C(u_{6})=\{a_{6},k,k+1,k+2\}
,

C(u_{7})=\{a_{7},k+3,k+4,k+5\}
.

When
7\not\in C(x_{1})
, first
vx_{1}\to 7
,
if
6\not\in C(x_{2})
, then
(vx_{2},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(6,k)
;
otherwise,
6\in C(x_{2})
.
Since
[k,k+5]\cup\{2\}\subseteq C(x_{2})
, there exists
\beta\in[8,k-1]\setminus C(x_{2})
.
Then
vx_{2}\to\beta
and
[k,k+5]\setminus C(u_{\beta})\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}uv
.

In the other case,
7\in C(x_{1})
and likewise,
6\in C(x_{2})
.
For some
i\in(\{3,4,5\}\cup[8,k-1])\setminus(C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2}))
,
if
G
contains no
(i,j)_{(u_{i},x_{1})}
-path for some
j\in[k,k+2]
,
then
(vx_{1},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(i,j)
;
if
G
contains no
(i,j)_{(u_{i},x_{2})}
-path for some
j\in[k+3,k+5]
,
then
(vx_{2},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(i,j)
.
Otherwise,
G
contains a
(i,j)_{(u_{i},x_{1})}
-path for each
j\in[k,k+2]
and a
(i,j)_{(u_{i},x_{2})}
-path for each
j\in[k+3,k+5]
.
Thus,
[k,k+5]\subseteq C(u_{i})
and
d(u_{i})\geq 7
.
It follows that
[8,k-1]\subseteq C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2})
.

(4.1.1.)
c(x_{1}x_{2})\in\{3,4,5\}
, say
c(x_{1}x_{2})=3
.
Since
2\times|[k,k+5]|+|\{1,3,6,7\}|+|\{2,3,6,7\}|+|[8,k-1]|\leq 2\Delta=2k
,
we have
k\geq 12
, a contradiction.

(4.1.2.)
c(x_{1}x_{2})\in[8,k-1]
, say
c(x_{1}x_{2})=8
.
Since
2\times|[k,k+5]|+|\{1,6,7,8\}|+|\{2,6,7,8\}|+|[9,k-1]|\leq 2\Delta=2k
,
we have
k\geq 11
.
It follows that
k=11
, and
C(x_{1})=\{1,6,7,8,9\}\cup[k,k+5]
,

C(x_{2})=\{2,6,7,8,10\}\cup[k,k+5]
.
Since
G
contains a
(3,j)_{(u_{3},x_{1})}
-path for each
j\in[k,k+2]
and a
(3,j)_{(u_{3},x_{2})}
-path for each
j\in[k+3,k+5]
,
thus
x_{1}x_{2}\to 3
reduces to the case where
8\not\in C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2})
.

(4.1.3.)
c(x_{1}x_{2})\in[k,k+5]
, say
c(x_{1}x_{2})=k
.
Since
k\leq 11
and
2\times|[k,k+5]|+|\{1,6,7\}|+|\{2,6,7\}|+|[8,k-1]|\leq 2\Delta=2k
, i.e.,
10+k\leq 2k
,
we have
|\{3,4,5\}\cap(C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2}))|\leq 1
.
Assuming w.l.o.g.
3,4\not\in C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2})
.
Then
(vx_{1},vx_{2},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(3,4,k)
.

Case 4.2.
\{a,b\}\cap S_{5^{-}}(u)\neq\emptyset
, assuming w.l.o.g.
a=6
,
b\in\{1,3\}
.

When
6\not\in C(x_{2})
,
since
[k,\Delta+5]\cup\{1,6\}\subseteq C(x_{1})
and
|[k,\Delta+5]\cup\{1,6\}|+|[7,k-1]|=\Delta+1
, there exists
\gamma\in[7,k-1]\setminus C(x_{1})
.
Then
(vx_{2},vx_{1})\to(6,\gamma)
reduces the proof to Case 4.1.

In the other case,
6\in C(x_{2})
.
Assume
[7,k-1]\setminus(C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2}))\neq\emptyset
, say
7\not\in C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2})
,
then
vx_{2}\to 7
reduces the proof to Case 4.1.
Now assume that
[7,k-1]\subseteq C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2})
.

One sees that
k\leq 11
and
2\times|[k,\Delta+5]|+|\{1,6\}|+|\{2,b,6\}|+|[7,k-1]|=2\Delta+10-k\leq 2\Delta
.

When
b=1
, it follows that
\{3,4,5\}\setminus(C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2}))\neq\emptyset
,
say
3\not\in C(x_{1})\cup C(x_{2})
,
thus
vx_{2}\to 3
reduces the proof to the case where
b\in\{3,4,5\}
.
Hence, it suffices to assume that
b=3
.

(4.2.1.)
c(x_{1}x_{2})\in\{4,5\}
, say
c(x_{1}x_{2})=4
.
Since
2\times|[k,\Delta+5]|+|\{1,4,6\}|+|\{2,3,4,6\}|+|[7,k-1]|=2\Delta+12-k\leq 2\Delta
,
we have
k\geq 12
, a contradiction.

(4.2.2.)
c(x_{1}x_{2})\in[7,k-1]
, say
c(x_{1}x_{2})=7
.
Since
2\times|[k,\Delta+5]|+|\{1,6,7\}|+|\{2,3,6,7\}|+|[8,k-1]|=2\Delta+11-k\leq 2\Delta
,
we have
k\geq 11
.
It follows that
k=11
, and
C(x_{1})=\{1,6,7,8,9\}\cup[11,\Delta+5]
,

C(x_{2})=\{2,3,6,7\}\cup[10,\Delta+5]
.
It follows from
3.1.0.4
that
|C(u_{6})\cap[11,\Delta+5]|\leq 3
.
Let
[14,\Delta+5]\cap C(u_{6})=\emptyset
.
Then
[14,\Delta+5]\subseteq B_{3}
.
Let
(vx_{1},vx_{2})\to(3,8)
.

If there exists an
i\in[14,\Delta+5]\setminus C(u_{8})
,
then
uv\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}i
.
Otherwise,
[14,\Delta+5]\subseteq C(u_{8})
and it follows from
3.1.0.4
that
\Delta=11
and
