For brief description, we introduce a few symbols used in
[
5
]
.
A
multiset
is a generalized set where a member can appear multiple times.
If an element
x
appears
t
times in the multiset
MS
, then we say that the
multiplicity
of
x
in
MS
is
t
, and write mult
{}_{MS}(x)=t
.
The
cardinality
of a finite multiset
MS
, denoted by
\|MS\|
, is defined as
\|MS\|=\sum_{x\in MS}
mult
{}_{MS}(x)=t
.
Let
MS_{1}
and
MS_{2}
be two multisets.
The
join
of
MS_{1}
and
MS_{2}
, denoted
MS_{1}\biguplus MS_{2}
, is a multiset that has all the members of
MS_{1}
and of
MS_{2}
.
For
x\in MS_{1}\biguplus MS_{2}
, mult
{}_{MS_{1}\biguplus MS_{2}}(x)=
mult
{}_{MS_{1}}(x)+
mult
{}_{MS_{2}}(x)
.
Clearly,
\|MS_{1}\biguplus MS_{2}\|=\|MS_{1}\|+\|MS_{2}\|
.

Lemma 12

If

can be obtained such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq a_{uv}-1
,
or the following holds:

(1)

for each
i\in A_{uv}
,

(1.1)

for each

,

H
contains a
(i_{j},j)_{(u_{i_{j}},v_{i_{j}})}
-path and a
(a_{i},j)_{(u_{i},u_{a_{i}})}
-path for some
i_{j}\in A_{uv}
,

, and
j\in C(u_{i_{j}})\cap C(u_{a_{i}})
;

(1.2)

for each

,

contains a
(i_{j},j)_{(u_{i_{j}},v_{i_{j}})}
-path and a

-path for some
i_{j}\in A_{uv}
,
b_{i}\in D_{i}\setminus\{i,i_{j}\}
, and
j\in C(v_{i_{j}})\cap C(v_{b_{i}})
;

(2)

for each
j\in T_{uv}
, mult
{}_{S_{u}}(j)\geq 2
and mult
{}_{S_{v}}(j)\geq 2
.

(3)

For each
\alpha\in U_{uv}
,
if there exists an

such that recoloring
uu_{\alpha}\to j
cannot obtain a bichromatic cycle,
then
\alpha\in\bigcup_{i\in A_{uv}}B_{i}
,

,

,

, or there exists a

-path.

(4)

For each
\beta\in V_{uv}
, if there exists an
j\in R_{\beta}
such that recoloring
vv_{\beta}\to j
cannot obtain a bichromatic cycle,
then
\beta\in\bigcup_{i\in A_{uv}}B_{i}
,

{}_{S_{u}}(\beta)\geq 2
,
and for each
i\in A_{uv}
,
\beta\in C(u_{i})
, or there exists a
a_{i}\in C(u_{i})
such that

contains a
(\beta,a_{i})_{(u_{a_{i}},u_{i})}
-path.

Proof.

For each
j\in T_{uv}
,
it follows from
j\in\bigcup_{i\in A_{uv}}B_{i}
that there exists an
i_{j}\in A_{uv}
such that
j\in B_{i_{j}}\subseteq C(u_{i_{j}})\cap C(v_{i_{j}})
.

When, for some

,
G
contains no
(j,l)_{(u_{i},u_{l})}
-path for each
l\in C(u)
,

uu_{i}\to j
reduces to the case where
|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq a_{uv}-1
.
In the other case, for each
j\in T_{i}\neq\emptyset
,

H
contains a
(a_{i},j)_{(u_{i},u_{a_{i}})}
-path for some
a_{i}\in C_{i}
,
and
j\in C(u_{a_{i}})
.
By Lemma
6
,
a_{i}\neq i_{j}
and
j\in C(u_{i_{j}})\cap C(u_{a_{i}})
.

When, for some

,
G
contains no
(j,l)_{(v_{i},v_{l})}
-path for each
l\in C(v)
,

vv_{i}\to j
reduces to the case where
|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq a_{uv}-1
.
In the other case, for each
j\in R_{i}\neq\emptyset
,

H
contains a
(b_{i},j)_{(v_{i},v_{b_{i}})}
-path for some
b_{i}\in D_{i}
,
and
j\in C(v_{b_{i}})
.
By Lemma
6
,
b_{i}\neq i_{j}
and thus
j\in C(v_{i_{j}})\cap C(v_{b_{i}})
.

For each
j\in T_{uv}
,
one sees clearly that if
j\in C(u_{i})
for each
i\in A_{uv}
, then mult
{}_{S_{u}}(j)\geq 2
;
if
j\not\in C(u_{i})
for some
i\in A_{uv}
, i.e.,
j\in T_{i}
, then by (1.1),
there exists
i_{j}\in A_{uv}
,
a_{i}\in C(u)\setminus\{i,i_{j}\}
such that
j\in C(u_{i_{j}})\cap C(u_{a_{i}})
.
Hence, mult
{}_{S_{u}}(j)\geq 2
.
Since recoloring
uu_{\alpha}\to j
reduces to another acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
such that
a_{uv}\geq 2
,
by the similar discussion to show (1) and (2), (3) holds.
Likewise, (4) holds.

\Box

Let
T_{0}=\{j\in T_{uv}\mid\mbox{mult}_{S_{u}}(j)\geq 3\}
, and
t_{0}=|T_{0}|
.
If
a_{uv}\geq 2
,
Then

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[1,k-1]}d(u_{i})=\sum_{i\in[1,k-1]}w_{i}+\sum_{j\in T_{uv}}mult_{S_{u}}(j)\geq\sum_{i\in[1,k-1]}w_{i}+2t_{uv}+\sum_{j\in T_{0}}(mult_{S_{u}}(j)-2).

Corollary 2

If
a_{uv}=4
, i.e.,
d(u)=5
, and
A_{uv}=[1,4]
,
then an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
can be obtained such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq 3
,
or the following holds:

(1)

for each
i\in A_{uv}
,
T_{i}\subseteq\bigcap_{j\in A_{uv}\setminus\{i\}}C(u_{j})
,
and
\bigcup_{j\in A_{uv}\setminus\{i\}}T_{j}\subseteq C(u_{i})
;

(2)

for each
i,j\in A_{uv}
,
T_{i}\cap T_{j}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

Assuming
j\in T_{1}
and w.l.o.g. that
H
contains a
(2,j)_{(u_{2},v)}
-path.
One sees that if
j\in T_{i}
for some
i\in[3,4]
, say
j\in T_{3}
,
then it follows from Lemma
12
that
H
contains a
(4,j)_{(u_{1},u_{4})}
-path and a
(4,j)_{(u_{3},u_{4})}
-path, a contradiction.
Hence,
j\in C(u_{3})
and (1), (2) hold.

\Box

Corollary 3

If

, i.e.,

-coloring for

{}_{\bigcup_{i\in A_{uv}}C(u_{i})}(i_{4})\geq 2

,

,

,

,

, or there exists a

for each

with

(6.1)

,

;

(6.2)

d(u)=5
with

-path for each

mult
{}_{S_{u}}(j)\geq 3
for each

,

Lemma 13

If
a_{uv}=4
, then in
O(1)
time either an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
can be obtained,
or an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
can be obtained such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq 3
.

Proof.

It suffices to assume
d(u)=5
and
c(uu_{i})=i
,
i\in[1,4]
.

Since
a_{uv}=4
and
|C(v)\setminus\{c(vu_{3}),c(vu_{4})\}\leq 3
,
assume w.l.o.g.
c(vu_{3})=i_{1}\in\{1,2,4\}
.
Since
d(v)\in[5,6]
,
t_{uv}\geq\Delta+5-5=\Delta
.
One sees clearly that
T_{i}\neq\emptyset
,
i\in[1,4]
, and
R_{i_{1}}\neq\emptyset
.

It follows from Lemma
12
(1.1) that for each
i\in[1,4]
and
j\in T_{i}
,
there exists a
a_{i}\in C_{i}\setminus\{i\}
such that
H
contains a
(a_{i},j)_{(u_{i},u_{a_{i}})}
-path,
and from Lemma
12
(1.2) that for each
j\in R_{i_{1}}=T_{3}
, there exists a
b_{i_{1}}\in C(u_{3})\setminus\{i_{1}\}
such that
H
contains a
(b_{i},j)_{(u_{3},v_{b_{i}})}
-path.
Thus,
a_{3}\neq b_{i_{1}}
by Lemma
6
.

Since
|T_{uv}|\geq\Delta
and
1,a_{1}\in C(u_{1})
, we have
t_{1}\geq 2
.
Likewise,
t_{2}\geq 2
,
t_{3}\geq 4
, and
t_{4}\geq 3
.

It follows from Corollary
2
that for each
i\in[1,4]
,

\bigcup_{j\in A_{uv}\setminus\{i\}}T_{j}\subseteq C(u_{i})
,
and for each
i,j,l\in A_{uv}
,
|T_{i}\cup T_{j}\cup T_{l}|=|T_{i}|+|T_{j}|+|T_{l}|=t_{i}+t_{j}+t_{l}
.

Then,
d(u_{1})\geq|\{1,a_{1}\}|+|T_{2}\cup T_{3}\cup T_{4}|=2+t_{2}+t_{3}+t_{4}\geq 2+2+4+3=11
,

d(u_{2})\geq|\{1,a_{2}\}|+|T_{3}\cup T_{4}\cup T_{1}|=2+t_{3}+t_{4}+t_{1}\geq 2+4+3+2=11
,

d(u_{3})\geq|\{3,i_{1},a_{3},b_{i_{1}}\}|+|T_{4}\cup T_{1}\cup T_{2}|=4+t_{4}+t_{1}+t_{2}\geq 4+3+2+2=11
,

d(u_{4})\geq|\{4,i_{2},a_{4}\}|+|T_{1}\cup T_{2}\cup T_{3}|=3+t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}\geq 3+2+2+4=11
,
and thus none of (
A_{7}
) and (
A_{8}
) holds.

\Box

Lemma 14

If
a_{uv}=3
, then in
O(1)
time either an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
can be obtained,
or an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
can be obtained such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq 2
.

Proof.

We discuss the following two cases for
d(u)=4
and
d(u)=5
, respectively.

Case 1.

d(u)=4
and
c(uu_{i})=i
,
i\in[1,3]=A_{uv}
.

In this case, assume
c(vv_{i})=i
,
i\in[1,d(v)-1]
,
and
t_{uv}=\Delta+5-(d(v)-1)=\Delta+6-d(v)
.

Assume that
vu_{2}\in E(G)
.
Since
t_{uv}\geq\Delta-1
and
2,c(vu_{2})\in C(u_{2})
,
we have
T_{2}\neq\emptyset
.
It follows from Corollary
3
(1.1) that for each
j\in T_{2}
,
there exists a
a_{2}\in\{1,3\}
such that
H
contains a
(a_{i},j)_{(u_{i},u_{a_{i}})}
-path.
When
c(vu_{2})=\alpha\in\{1,3\}
, let
\beta=\{1,3\}\setminus\{\alpha\}
,
thus it follows from Corollary
3
(1.1) that for each
j\in T_{2}
,

H
contains a

-path and a
(\beta,j)_{(u_{\beta},v_{\beta})}
-path,
a contradiction.
In the other case, if
vu_{2}\in E(G)
, then
T_{2}\neq\emptyset
, for each
j\in T_{2}
,
there exists a
a_{2}\in\{1,3\}
such that
H
contains a
(a_{i},j)_{(u_{i},u_{a_{i}})}
-path and
c(vu_{2})\not\in A_{uv}
.

Case 1.1.
d(v)\leq 6
.
Then
t_{uv}\geq\Delta
.
It follows that for each
i\in[1,3]
,
T_{i}\neq\emptyset
,
and from Corollary
3
(1.1) that for each

and

Further, by Corollary

(7), mult
{}_{S_{u}}(j)\geq 3

,
or if there exists a

.
One sees that

,
and

.
Hence,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in N(u)}d(x)

\displaystyle\geq 2\times t_{uv}+\sum_{i\in[1,3]}|\{1,a_{i}\}|+|V_{uv}|+d(v)

\displaystyle=2\times(\Delta+6-d(v))+2\times 3+d(v)-4+d(v)=2\Delta+14.

and thus (
A_{6.3}
) holds, i.e.,
uv
is contained in two
3
-cycles
uvu_{2}
,
uvu_{3}
and
\Sigma_{x\in N(u)}d(x)=2\Delta+14
.

Since
vu_{2},vu_{3}\in E(G)
,
c(vu_{2}),c(vu_{2})\not\in[1,3]
,
i.e.,
1,2,3\in C(v)\setminus\{c(vu_{2}),c(vu_{3})\}
.
It follows that
d(v)=6
and assume
(vu_{2},vu_{3})_{c}=(4,5)
.
Further, we have

;

,
a_{3}\in\{1,2\}
.

Let
\gamma=\{1,3\}\setminus\{a_{2}\}
.
One sees clearly that

,

,
and for each

,

-path and a

Thus,

and

.

Case 1.2.
d(v)=7
and (
A_{6.4}
) holds.
Since

,

,

Since

, there exists a

{}_{S_{u}}(j)=2
.

(4) that

.

, mult
{}_{S_{u}}(i)\leq 2

and there exists

{}_{S_{u}}(i)=

{}_{S_{u}}(j)=1

,

,

,

,
and

Assuming w.l.o.g.
(u_{1}u_{2},u_{1}u_{3})_{c}=(7,8)
.
Since mult

By Lemmas
13
,
14
and
15
,
hereafter we assume that
a_{uv}=2
.
It suffices to show that in
O(1)
time an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
G
can be obtained,
or an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
can be obtained such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq 1
.

Corollary 4

If
a_{uv}=2
, i.e.,
A_{uv}=\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
and
U_{uv}=\{i_{3},\ldots,i_{k-1}\}
,
then either an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
can be obtained such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq 1
,
or the following holds:

(1)

for each
j\in T_{i_{1}}\neq\emptyset
, mult
{}_{S_{u}}(j)\geq 2
,
G
contains a
(i_{2},j)_{(u_{i_{2}},v)}
-path, and

(1.1)

if
d(u)=4
, then
i_{3}\in C(u_{i_{1}})
,
G
contains a
(i_{3},j)_{(u_{i_{1}},u_{i_{3}})}
-path,
and
T_{i_{1}}\subseteq C(u_{i_{3}})
;

(1.2)

if
d(u)=5
, then
U_{uv}=\{i_{3},i_{4}\}
, there exists a
a_{i_{1}}\in\{i_{3},i_{4}\}\cap C(u_{i_{1}})
such that

G
contains a
(a_{i_{1}},j)_{(u_{i_{1}},u_{a_{i_{1}}})}
-path,
j\in C(u_{a_{i_{1}}})
, and
T_{i_{1}}\subseteq C(u_{i_{3}})\cup C(u_{i_{4}})
;

(2)

T_{i_{1}}\subseteq C(u_{i_{2}})
,
T_{i_{2}}\subseteq C(u_{i_{1}})
,
and
T_{i_{1}}\cap T_{i_{2}}=\emptyset
.

(3)

For each
\alpha\in U_{uv}
,
if there exists an
j\in T_{\alpha}
such that
uu_{\alpha}\to j
cannot obtain a bichromatic cycle,
then
\alpha\in\bigcup_{i\in\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}B_{i}
,
mult
{}_{S_{u}}(\alpha)\geq 2
,
and for each
i\in\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
,
\alpha\in C(u_{i})
, or there exists a
a_{i}\in C(u_{i})\cap\{i_{3},i_{4}\}
such that
H
contains a
(\alpha,a_{i})_{(u_{\alpha},u_{i})}
-path.

(4)

For each
\beta\in V_{uv}
,
if there exists an
j\in R_{\beta}
such that

for each

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
12
(1.1) that (1–2) holds,
and from Lemma
12
(3–4) that (3–4) hold.

For (5), assume that
T_{i_{2}}\cup(\{i_{3}\}\setminus C(u_{i_{2}}))\neq\emptyset
.
If
i_{1}\not\in C(u_{i_{3}})
and
H
contains no
(i,i_{1})_{(u_{i_{3}},u_{i})}
-path for each
i\in C(u)
,
then
(uu_{i_{1}},uu_{i_{3}})\to(j,i_{1})
and
uv\to(T_{2}\cup\{i_{3}\})\setminus C(u_{2})
.
Otherwise,
i_{1}\in C(u_{i_{3}})
or
H
contains a
(i,i_{1})_{(u_{i_{3}},u_{i})}
-path for some

.
It follows that

.

For each

, if

,
and

contains no
(i,l)_{(u_{i_{1}},u_{i})}
-path for each

,
one sees that the same argument applies if

and it follows that there exists an
j\in C(u)
such that

contains a
(j,l)_{(u_{i_{3}},u_{j})}
-path.

,
or there exists an

such that

contains a
(i,l)_{(u_{i_{1}},u_{i})}
-path,

and then

.
Hence, (5.1) holds.

Assume

with

.

{}_{S_{u}}(i_{4})\geq 2

Recall that Eq.(

By Corollary
5
(6), we proceed with the following proposition:

Proposition 3.2.2.1

If
T_{i_{2}}\neq\emptyset
and
C(v_{i_{1}})\setminus\{i_{1}\}\subseteq T_{uv}
,
or

-coloring for

can be obtained in

Corollary 6

If
d(u)=5
,
a_{uv}=2
, and
T_{i_{1}}\setminus C(u_{i_{4}})\neq\emptyset
,
T_{i_{2}}\setminus C(u_{i_{3}})\neq\emptyset
,
then either an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for
H
can be obtained such that
|C(u)\cap C(v)|\leq 1
,
or
H
contains a
(T_{i_{1}}\cap T_{i_{4}},T_{i_{2}}\cap T_{i_{3}})_{(u_{i_{1}},u_{i_{3}})}
-path,
and a
(T_{i_{2}}\cap T_{i_{3}},T_{i_{1}}\cap T_{i_{4}})_{(u_{i_{2}},u_{i_{4}})}
-path.

Proof.

One sees that
H
contains a
(i_{3},T_{i_{1}})_{(u_{i_{1}},u_{i_{3}})}
-path and a
(i_{4},T_{i_{2}})_{(u_{i_{2}},u_{i_{4}})}
-path.
If
H
contains no
(\alpha,\beta)_{(u_{i_{1}},u_{i_{3}})}
-path for some
\alpha\in T_{i_{1}}\cap T_{i_{4}}
,

\beta\in T_{i_{2}}\cap T_{i_{3}}
, then
(uu_{i_{1}},uu_{i_{3}})\to(\alpha,\beta)
reduces to an acyclic edge
(\Delta+5)
-coloring for

such that

.
Otherwise,
H
contains a
(T_{i_{1}}\cap T_{i_{4}},T_{i_{2}}\cap T_{i_{3}})_{(u_{i_{1}},u_{i_{3}})}
-path,
and a
(T_{i_{2}}\cap T_{i_{3}},T_{i_{1}}\cap T_{i_{4}})_{(u_{i_{2}},u_{i_{4}})}
-path.

\Box

We prove the inductive step by first treating
u_{3},u_{4}
indistinguishably to obtain an acyclic edge
(\Delta+2)
-coloring for
G
,
until impossible, by then (after we discuss the detailed configurations) to distinguish
u_{3}
and
u_{4}
.
Hereafter, for each
i,j\in[1,4]
, let

,

.

Below we distinguish two subcases where

and
6
, respectively.

Case 1.

d(v)=5
.
Then
t_{uv}=\Delta-1
,
V_{uv}=[5,6]
.

Since
T_{uv}\subseteq C(u_{i_{1}})\cup C(u_{i_{2}})
,
one sees that if
\{i,c(vu_{i})\}=\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
, then
T_{i}\neq\emptyset
,
thus, for each
i\in[3,4]
,
if
i\in A_{uv}
, then
c(vu_{i})\not\in A_{uv}
,
and it follows from Corollary
4
(1.2) that there exists a
a_{i}\in\{i_{3},i_{4}\}\cap C(u_{i})
such that
H
contains a
(a_{i},j)_{(u_{i},u)}
-path for some
j\in T_{i}
;
if
c(vu_{i})=l\in A_{uv}
, then
i\not\in A_{uv}
,
and it follows from Lemma

Lemma 22

Hence, we proceed with the following proposition, or otherwise we are done:

Proposition 3.2.2.48

Assume that
5\not\in C(u_{4})
. Then

(1)

if
5\not\in C(u_{1})
,
H
contains no
(5,2)_{(u_{2},u_{4})}
-path,
then
3\in C(u_{1})
,
H
contains a
(3,T_{1})_{(u_{1},u_{3})}
-path;

(2)

if
5\not\in C(u_{2})
,
H
contains no
(5,1)_{(u_{1},u_{4})}
-path,
then
3\in C(u_{2})
,
H
contains a
(3,T_{2})_{(u_{2},u_{3})}
-path;

(3)

if
H
contains no
(5,1)_{(u_{4},u_{1})}
-path,
then
4\in C(u_{2})
or
H
contains a
(4,1/3)_{(u_{2},u)}
-path;

(4)

if
H
contains no
(5,2)_{(u_{4},u_{2})}
-path,
then
4\in C(u_{1})
or
H
contains a
(4,2/3)_{(u_{1},u)}
-path;

(5)

if
H
contains no
(5,i)_{(u_{4},u_{i})}
-path for each
i\in[1,2]
,
then
4\in B_{1}\cup B_{2}
, mult
{}_{S_{u}}(4)\geq 2
, and for each
i\in[1,2]
,
4\in C(u_{i})
,
or
H
contains a
(3,4)_{(u_{i},u_{3})}
-path,
3\in C(u_{i})
,
4\in C(u_{3})
.

For each
j\in[6,7]\setminus C(u_{4})
, let

Lemma 30

w_{1}\geq 3
and
t_{1}=\Delta-d(u_{1})+w_{1}-2\geq w_{1}-2\geq 1
.

Proof.

w_{1}\leq 2
.

.

.

(\{1,6,7\},T_{4})_{(u_{1},u)}
-path.

(2) that

Lemma 39

(5,T_{1}\cap T_{3})_{(u_{1},u_{4})}
-path.
If
1\not\in C(u_{4})
and
H
contains no
(1,3)_{(u_{3},u_{4})}
-path,
then
(uu_{1},uu_{2},uu_{4})\to(T_{1}\cap T_{3},5,1)
reduces the proof to Case 2.4.2.
Otherwise,
1\in C(u_{4})
or
H
contains a
(1,3)_{(u_{3},u_{4})}
-path.
Likewise, if mult
{}_{S_{u}\setminus C(u_{4})}(6)=1
, then
6\in C(u_{2})
,
2\in C(u_{1})
,
and
2\in C(u_{3})
or
H
contains a
(2,4)_{(u_{3},u_{4})}
-path.

Then
\sum_{x\in N(u)\setminus\{v\}}d(x)\geq+2\Delta+11+t_{0}+|\{1,6/2\}|=2\Delta+13+t_{0}
.
It follows that
t_{0}=0
,
(C_{12}\cup T_{2})\cap C(u_{4})=\emptyset
,
(C_{12}\cup T_{1})\cap C(u_{3})=\emptyset
,
and mult
{}_{S_{u}}(i)=2
,
i=3,4
, and mult
{}_{S_{u}}(7)=1
.

One sees that
5\not\in C(u_{4})
and
C_{12}\cap C(u_{3})=\emptyset
.
Now we want to show that for some
j^{*}\in T_{2}\cup C_{12}\cup\{3\}
,

H
contains no
(i,j)_{(u_{4},v)}
-path for each
i\in 1,2,7
.

Assume that
H
contains a
(7,j)_{(u_{4},v_{7})}
-path for some
j\in T_{2}
.
If
H
contains no
(4,7)_{(u_{1},u_{4})}
-path,
then
(uu_{1},uv)\overset{\divideontimes}{\to}(7,j)
.
Otherwise,
H
contains a
(4,7)_{(u_{1},u_{4})}
-path.
Thus, by
3.2.2.50
(2), we are done.
Hence,
H
contains no
(7,j)_{(u_{4},v_{7})}
-path for each
j\in T_{2}
.

Further, we assume that
H
contains a
(2,T_{2})_{(u_{4},v_{2})}
-path.
If
3\in C(v_{2})
, one sees from
3.2.2.78
(1) that
C_{12}\setminus C(v_{2})\neq\emptyset
,
and it follows from Lemma
12
(1.2) that
H
contains a
(7,C_{12}\cap R_{2})_{(v_{2},v_{7})}
-path.
Then
j^{*}\in C_{12}\cap R_{2}
.
Otherwise,
3\not\in C(v_{2})
.
Since
3\in B_{1}
,
3\in C(u_{1})\setminus C(u_{4})
.
One sees from
3.2.2.57
(2) that
H
contains a
(3,7)_{(v_{2},v_{7})}
-path.
Then
j^{*}=3
.
Hence, by Corollary
4
(4) or
vu_{4}\to 3
,
we have
6\in B_{1}\cup B_{2}
, and mult
{}_{S_{u}}(6)=2
.
It follows that mult
{}_{S_{u}}(2)=1
.

One sees from
3.2.2.80
(2.2) that
2\in C(u_{1})
or
H
contains a
(2,3/4)_{(u_{1},u)}
-path.
Together with
2\in C(u_{3})
or
H
contains a
(2,1/4)_{(u_{3},u)}
-path,
2\in C(u_{1})\cup C(u_{3})
.
If
2\in C(u_{2})
, one sees that
1\in C(u_{3})
,
3\in C(u_{4})
,
then
(uu_{1},uu_{2},uu_{3},uu_{4})\to(3,T_{2},2,1)
reduces the proof to Case 2.3.
Otherwise,
2\in C(u_{3})
and then
3\in C(u_{1})
,
1\in C(u_{4})
.
One sees from
3.2.2.49
(1) that
7\in C(u_{4})
,
and from
3.2.2.50
(2) that
H
contains a
(4,7)_{(u_{3},u_{4})}
-path.
Then
(uu_{1},uu_{2})\to(7,5)
reduces the proof to Case (2.5.1.)
This finishes the inductive step for the Case (2.5.1.)

This finishes the inductive step for the case where
G
contains the configuration (
A_{6}
)–(
A_{8}
),
and thus completes the proof of Theorem
1
.

4
Conclusions

Proving the acyclic edge coloring conjecture (AECC) on general graphs has a long way to go,
but on planar graphs it is very close to reach with recent efforts.
In particular, the AECC has been confirmed true for planar graphs without
i
-cycles, for each
i\in\{3,4,5,6\}
,
and for planar graphs that containing no intersecting
3
-cycles.
In this paper, we deal with the general planar graph.
Prior to our work, their acyclic chromatic index was shown to be at most
\Delta+6
,
and in this work we made another step that
a^{\prime}(G)\leq\Delta+5
.
The main technique is a discharging method to show that at least one of the several specific local structures must exist in the graph,
followed by an induction on the number of edges in the graph.

The discharging method is classic;
different from the previous work, we do not always validate
\omega^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
for every element in
x\in V(H)\cup F(H)
independently,
but sometimes collectively for subsets of elements.
For example, in Discharging-Lemma
3
we validate
\sum_{x\in\{v\}\cup F_{3}(v)}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 0
,
and in Discharging-Lemma
4
, when
u
is a
4
-vertex in
V(H)
adjacent to a vertex
v
,
we validate
\sum_{x\in\{u,v\}\cup(F_{3}(u)\cup F_{3}(v))}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 0
if
d_{H}(v)=4
,
and
\sum_{x\in\{u\}\cup F_{3}(u)}\omega^{\prime}(x)\leq 0
if
d_{H}(v)\geq 5
.

Some notations defined and some properties unearthed in the paper are of importance during the proof.
For example, the notations of
T_{i}
,
W_{i}
,
C_{ij}
,
S_{u}
,
T_{0}
,
and if
C(u)\cap C(v)=\{1,2\}
, then
C(u_{1})=W_{1}\cup T_{2}\cup C_{12}
,
C(u_{2})=W_{2}\cup T_{1}\cup C_{12}
,
and the characteristic stated in Lemma
12
, Corollary
4
and Corollary
5
.

Surely, our detailed construction and proofs are built on top of several existing works,
and we believe our new design of the local structures and the associated discharging method can be helpful
for further lowering the upper bound of the acyclic chromatic number on planar graphs, or addressing the AECC eventually.
On the other hand, for general graphs, improvements from the approximation algorithm perspective,
that is, to reduce the coefficient for
\Delta
in the upper bound (from the currently best
3.74
), are impactful pursuit.
